# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/)
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### BREAKING: choice-field options follow Nova's order

`Select::options()` and `MultiSelect::options()` now read `[value => label]`, the order Nova uses: the array key is stored, the array value is shown. v1.x read the array label first, so `options(fn () => User::pluck('name', 'id')->all())` stored the name and not the id, and the examples in these docs written in Nova's order (`pluck('name', 'id')`, `['csv' => 'CSV']`) stored the label. Nothing fails at runtime when an array keeps the v1 order, so every call needs a look. Upgrade steps:

1. Require the new major: `composer require martis/martis:^2.0` (a `^1.x` constraint never installs it).
2. Flip every associative array written label first: `['Draft' => 'draft']` becomes `['draft' => 'Draft']`, `pluck('id', 'name')` becomes `pluck('name', 'id')`.
3. Turn lists into maps: a list is keyed 0, 1, 2…, as in Nova, so `['draft', 'published']` now stores `0` and `1`. Pass `array_combine($values, $values)` when each value is its own label, or pass an enum class. A list of numbers (`[1, 2, 3]`, `range(1, 12)`) shifts by one value with no error: a stored 1 shows as "2".
4. Rewrite MultiSelect groups in Nova's format: `['Backend' => ['PHP' => 'php']]` becomes `['php' => ['label' => 'PHP', 'group' => 'Backend']]`. The old format throws an `InvalidArgumentException` naming the field and the option.
5. Replace `optionsFromMap()` with `options()`.
6. Return `[value => label]` from `searchOptionsUsing()` closures: a list stores positions that change with the search term.
7. Review what else reads a flipped array. Filters keep `[label => value]`: a `SelectFilter` that shared the field's array (for example `Post::STATUSES`) now shows the value as the label and filters on the label, with zero results and no error; give it `array_flip()` of the array, or its own array. A `Rule::in(array_values(...))` rule now lists the labels (use `array_keys()`), and Badge maps and `displayUsing()` lookups built from the array are now keyed by value.
8. In a `BulkAssignRole` action generated by `martis:roles` (and in a published `stubs/martis/roles-bulk-assign-role-action.stub`), change `pluck('id', 'name')` to `pluck('name', 'id')`. Regenerate agent primers written by `martis:agents` under v1.x (`php artisan martis:agents --force`): they recommend `options($enum::values())`.
9. Look for records saved with the wrong order: reading one logs a warning (static options).
10. In each environment, after deploying the flipped arrays: `php artisan martis:publish-assets` (the grouped dropdown and the `''` option fix ship in the frontend bundle), then `php artisan martis:cache:clear schema` (it keeps the old options for up to a day, or with no expiration under an empty `MARTIS_CACHE_SCHEMA_TTL`; cleared before the deploy, the next page caches the old options again), and `php artisan martis:cache:prune` on the database and file stores.
11. Relationship writes need the related resource's `viewAny`, and Through relationships offer no Create: see Security and Changed below, and [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md).

`grep -rnF -e '->options(' -e 'optionsFromMap(' -e 'searchOptionsUsing(' app/ Modules/ packages/` (plus any other source folder) finds the calls. See [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md).

### Security

- **A relationship panel returned every row for a negative `per_page`.** The `has-many`, `morph-many`, `belongs-to-many` and `morph-to-many` lists and the two attach searches capped `?per_page=` at 100 but not below it. Laravel ignores a negative limit, so on PostgreSQL and SQL Server `?per_page=-1` returned every row of the relation (on an attach search, every attachable record) in one response, which any user who could view the parent could repeat to exhaust the server's memory; MySQL and SQLite answered 500. The page size is now clamped between 1 and 100, as the resource index and the lenses already were, so `per_page=0` or a non-numeric value now lists 1 row instead of the model's default 15. +4 Pest.
- **A `HasManyThrough` panel showed, and acted on, the intermediate record's id.** The `has-many` index paginated the relation's bare query, which on a `hasManyThrough` selects every column of the join, so the intermediate's `id`, timestamps and `deleted_at` overwrote the listed record's: a row showed another record's id, its View / Edit / Delete / Restore / Force delete targeted that record (a Force delete could permanently remove an active record of another parent), and a trashed record came back with `deleted_at: null` when the intermediate soft-deletes. It happened on 1.x for View, Restore and Force delete (fixed there in v1.39.2). The index now paginates through the relation, which selects only the related table's columns, so the panel's sort needs no qualified column (an alias such as a `withCount` column keeps sorting), except a JSON path (`meta->code`), which the `hasManyThrough`, `belongsToMany` and `morphToMany` panels qualify. The `hasManyThrough`, `belongsToMany` and `morphToMany` panels qualify the columns they search by, which the intermediate or the pivot made ambiguous when it has the same column; the other panels and the resource index keep their search columns as written, since their relation or `indexQuery()` may join the table a searchable field reads. A `belongsToMany`, `morphToMany` or `hasManyThrough` relation that joins another table itself can therefore no longer be searched by a column only that table has. +8 Pest.
- **A write through a relationship ignored the related resource's `viewAny`.** `POST`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on the `has-many`, `has-one`, `morph-many` and `morph-one` endpoints checked the parent's `viewAny` / `view` and the related record's `create` / `update` / `delete`, but not the related resource's `viewAny`, which its own per-id endpoints require since v1.34.0 and Nova requires too: a user who could not list `Comment` still wrote comments through a post's panel. Those writes now answer 403 first, and a panel whose related resource denies `viewAny` no longer offers Create, Edit, Delete, Restore or Force delete (it still lists its records, as in 1.x: Nova 1 to 3 hid such a panel and Nova 4/5 does not document it). `routable()` is not required, so a headless resource (v1.24.0) keeps working as a relation target. **Upgrading from 1.x:** a user whose policy denies `viewAny` on the related resource can no longer create, edit or delete its records through a relationship panel or its endpoints; grant `viewAny` and confine the listing with `indexQuery()` if that user should. +26 Pest. See [Authorization → `viewAny` is the entry gate](docs/authorization.md#viewany-is-the-entry-gate).
- **A metric's cached result was served to every user.** Both cache paths of `Metric::resolve()` (the `metrics` layer and a per-class `cacheFor()`) keyed the entry on the metric, range, filters and locale only, so a `calculate()` scoped to the user, their tenant or their permissions served the first user's value to everyone for the TTL (5 minutes by default). The key now carries the authenticated user (`Metric::resultCacheKey()`); guests share one entry. Laravel Nova keys its metric cache without the user; Martis does not follow it here because the leak is a data exposure. Effect on an existing installation: no previous metric entry is reused (every key changes, so each user's first request computes the metric again and the old entries expire by their TTL), and a metric that is the same for everyone is now computed and stored once per user, so the cache holds more entries and hits less often; raise `MARTIS_CACHE_METRICS_TTL` or the `cacheFor()` lifetime for heavy global metrics. See [Cache](docs/cache.md#the-four-built-in-layers). +6 Pest (`MetricCacheUserScopeTest`: the two "each user gets their own value" cases fail on the previous key).
- **A create through a Through relationship filed the record under another parent.** `HasManyController` resolved a `hasManyThrough` relationship for its store as for a plain `hasMany`: `POST /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/has-many/{relationship}` answered 201 after writing the parent's key into the relationship's second key (the project's `client_id` for the Playground's `TeamMember::managedProjects()`), which files the record under whichever intermediate has that id (another team member's client), or failed on the foreign key when none has. It was reachable through a `HasManyThrough` field (with `canCreate()`, or by calling the endpoint), through a plain `HasMany` field declared on a `hasManyThrough` relationship (which offered Create by default) and, through the `has-one` endpoints, through a plain `HasOne` (or `HasOneOfMany`) field declared on a `hasOneThrough` one. A create through a Through relationship now answers 403 (`Records cannot be created through a hasManyThrough relationship.` / `… a hasOneThrough relationship.`), whether the field or the Eloquent relationship is the Through one. **Upgrading from 1.x:** create those records from their own resource, and if a panel offered Create on a Through relationship, check the second key of the records created from it. +5 Pest. See [Relationships → Upgrading the Through fields from 1.x](docs/relationships.md#upgrading-the-through-fields-from-1x).

### Changed

- **The `schema` cache layer expires after a day by default (`MARTIS_CACHE_SCHEMA_TTL=1440`), `martis:cache:prune` deletes the entries older versions left behind, and a cache key past 191 characters is hashed.** Now that every key carries the installed version, each upgrade leaves the previous version's entries behind in every layer. Redis and memcached drop an expired orphan on their own; the `database` store (Laravel's default since 11) and the `file` store never do, because Laravel deletes an expired entry only when its key is read again. `php artisan martis:cache:prune` deletes, on the database store, the `martis:cache:*` rows that are expired or no longer carry a live version and counter, and on the file store every expired entry; other stores need nothing. A key past 191 characters once the store's own prefix is counted (a `utf8mb4`-indexed database cache column; memcached stops at 250 bytes) is written as `martis:cache:{type}:h:{sha256}` of the whole key. **Upgrading from 1.x:** an empty `MARTIS_CACHE_SCHEMA_TTL=` (the `.env` block the 1.x docs showed) and a `config/martis.php` published before v2.0 keep "no expiration": set `MARTIS_CACHE_SCHEMA_TTL=1440`, and on the database or file store run `php artisan martis:cache:prune` after each upgrade (or schedule it). +9 Pest. See [Cache → Invalidation](docs/cache.md#invalidation).
- **A theme is edited in its source, then published.** The `martis:theme` hint and the theming guide said to edit `public/vendor/martis/themes/<name>.css`, and every publish deleted those edits. `resources/css/martis/<name>.css` is now the only place a theme lives: edit it, then run `php artisan martis:publish-assets --themes-only` (a full publish includes the themes too). A publish replaces the published copy with its source, also with `--no-wipe`, which used to leave the copy alone; a copy edited in place is backed up first. The header of a scaffolded theme says the same, instead of "save, and reload". A publish that would take away the theme `martis.theme.name` names, or that finds a theme source it cannot read, now stops with exit code 1 and changes nothing, so a deploy or `martis:install` in that state fails until the theme is in `resources/css/martis/`. `martis:theme:diff` compares the source, and for a theme that only has a published copy it fails and says to move the file there. If you edited a published copy on 1.x, copy it over the source and commit the source before you upgrade: see [Theming → Upgrading from 1.x](docs/theming.md#upgrading-from-1x).
- **Test hygiene for the theme specs.** The `martis:theme` specs rewrote `theme.name` in the testbench app's published `config/martis.php` (the `martis:install` specs publish one) and left it there, so every later spec of the run read a scaffolded theme name. `ThemeMakeCommandTest`, `ThemeScaffoldDiffTest` and the publish spec that scaffolds a theme restore the file through a shared `preservePublishedMartisConfig()` helper in `tests/Pest.php`.
- **`profile.resource` set to something that is not a profile resource throws.** A misspelt class fell back to the default resource without a word; an `InvalidArgumentException` now names the key and the reason, as the callable config keys do since 1.39.1. `null` or an empty string keeps the default.
- **Every initials avatar has the same letters and colour, and the theme sets the colour.** The Topbar (both layouts) showed one letter on the accent colour and the profile page its own initials on a fixed indigo, while the `Avatar` and `UiAvatar` fields painted a hex from a PHP palette that did not match the `--martis-avatar-N` tokens, so a theme could not recolour any of them. `Martis\Support\Initials` now gives all four the first letters of the first and last words and a slot of the theme's 16 `--martis-avatar-N` tokens, from the hash `avatarColorForSeed()` already used (its colours do not change); the frontend paints `var(--martis-avatar-{slot})`. **This changes how avatars look:** the Topbar shows two letters on a palette colour instead of one on the accent, the profile avatar leaves indigo, and a field's initials can land on another colour. `/api/auth/user`, the login response and `/api/profile` carry `avatar_initials` and `avatar_palette` (Martis adds them when a custom profile resource does not). The fields' initials payload keeps `initials`, `color` and `seed` and adds `palette`; `color` is the slot's colour in the built-in theme, or the `colorFrom()` value with `palette: null`. A Topbar picture that fails to load falls back to the initials instead of an empty circle. The trait's protected `resolveInitialsColor()`, `deterministicInitialsColor()` and `$initialsPalette` are gone: use `Martis\Support\Initials`. The docs had described the palette as shared with the Topbar and profile page, mentioned a login-page avatar that does not exist, and read a `user.initials` key the user object does not have. +32 Pest, +31 Vitest.
- **BREAKING: `Select` and `MultiSelect` read `options()` in Nova's order, `[value => label]`**, lists included (keyed 0, 1, 2…). `searchOptionsUsing()` returns the same shapes in the same order. The two fields share one normaliser (`Martis\Fields\Concerns\HasChoiceOptions`), so they accept the same forms. A numeric-string key is stored as the integer PHP makes of it, an empty-string key is a real option and a null label reads as empty. +12 Pest. See [Fields → Option order](docs/fields.md#option-order).

- **`HasOneThrough` and `HasManyThrough` work like Nova's: no Create, and their base field's other actions.** A Through panel never offers Create, as in Nova: `canCreate()` has no effect and stays callable so a resource that calls it keeps loading, and `canCreate(true)` logs a warning naming the field (`Log::warning`, on the default channel, once per field and request) rather than failing silently. Edit and Delete now show by default, as on `HasOne` / `HasMany` (they started hidden), Restore and Force delete on `HasManyThrough` trashed rows do not change (1.x already showed them) and now follow each record's policy like every row action, and the `has-one` endpoints take an update or a delete through a `HasOneThrough` field under the related resource's policies (1.x answered 403). An update or a delete through a Through relationship reaches only a record the relationship holds (404 for any other). **Upgrading from 1.x:** to keep the 1.x panel, add `->canUpdate(false)->canDelete(false)`, and deny `update` / `delete` in the related resource's policy to refuse those writes; drop `canCreate()` from Through fields; on a path repository, also clear the schema cache (`php artisan martis:cache:clear schema`). +23 Pest, +2 Vitest.

### Removed

- **BREAKING: `Select::optionsFromMap()`.** `options()` reads the same `[value => label]` map and also takes a closure.
- **BREAKING: the `['Group' => ['Label' => 'value']]` group format of `MultiSelect::options()`.** It throws an `InvalidArgumentException` naming the field and the option; use Nova's grouped options.

### Added

- **`martis:publish-assets --themes-only`** runs the theme step of a publish alone, without the wipe and the package assets (about 1400 files): the command for the edit-and-publish loop. It backs up and removes files like a full publish; with `--no-wipe` it also keeps the files without a source. The `martis:theme` hint and the header of a scaffolded theme point at it. See [Theming → Theme files](docs/theming.md#theme-files).
- **Grouped options in Nova's format on `Select` and `MultiSelect`**: `options(['MS' => ['label' => 'Small', 'group' => 'Men Sizes']])`. The `Select` dropdown shows each group under its heading (ungrouped options mixed in sit on top, with no heading); every option carries its `group` in the payload, and the remote search keeps it. +8 Vitest.
- **`MultiSelect::options(Status::class)`** takes an enum class, like `Select`: a backed case stores its value, a pure case its name. +1 Pest.
- **A warning for values stored in the pre-v2 order.** Reading a record whose `Select` or `MultiSelect` value matches the label of a static option and the value of none logs at most once per request for each model class and field, in production too: saving such a record again would store the wrong value. For options given as a list, a stored value that is the label of another option warns too (`options([1, 2, 3])` holding a 1 saved under v1.x, which now shows as "2"); lists of words and `range(0, n)` never do. The check reads the stored value, before `resolveUsing()`, and skips computed fields and a `Select` with `allowCustomValues()`. Options from a closure are not checked, so the check never runs a query of its own. Without a request, each field instance warns once; a fresh request (Octane) warns again. +17 Pest.
- **A warning for a `searchOptionsUsing()` closure that returns a list**, at most once per field per request: the field would store a position in that search's results. +2 Pest.
- **`withoutOptionOrderWarnings()` on `Select` and `MultiSelect`**, to silence the plain stored-label warning, the list-shift warning and the `searchOptionsUsing()` list warning for a field whose data is correct on purpose. A field can legitimately store a valid 0-based position that also reads as another option's label, for example a Nova rating that stores `0..4` and shows `"1".."5"`; without this the warning fires on every request even though nothing is wrong. +4 Pest. See [Fields → Option order](docs/fields.md#option-order).
- `options()` rejects a string that is not an enum class, and a label that cannot be a string, with an `InvalidArgumentException` naming the field.
- **`options()` takes a Collection or any other iterable** (`options(User::query()->pluck('name', 'id'))`), as Nova 5's `options(iterable|callable|string)` does, and any `Arrayable`; `options()` and `searchOptionsUsing()` closures may return one too. A subclass that overrides `options()` must accept `iterable|Arrayable|string|\Closure`. +5 Pest.

### Fixed

- **The Delete of a `HasOneOfMany` / `MorphOneOfMany` card removed another record than the one it showed.** With `latestByTimestamp()` or `oldestByTimestamp()` over a `hasMany` / `morphMany`, the card showed the record that order picks, but `DELETE …/has-one/{relationship}` (and `…/morph-one/{relationship}`), and a `PUT` sent straight to those routes, wrote `$relation->first()`, the first row in storage order: the card's Delete on the newest note deleted the oldest one. The card's Edit opens the related resource's own edit page with the shown id, so it was not affected. Show, update and destroy now read the same record through the relation, and the related resource's policies run on it. An Eloquent `latestOfMany()` / `ofMany()` relation was not affected. If you use these cards, check the records deleted from them: the deleted row may be a different one than the card showed (the first row stored).
- **A one-of-many card over a `hasOneThrough` showed and counted another parent's records.** With `latestByTimestamp()` or `oldestByTimestamp()`, the card showed the intermediate record's id, so its Edit opened a record of another parent. With those or with an Eloquent one-of-many relation (`latestOfMany()`, `oldestOfMany()`, `ofMany()`), its "1 of N" count and `aggregateVia()` tile matched the intermediate table's ids, so they counted and summed another parent's rows. The card now reads the record through the relation. The count and the tile go through the relation itself, so on a `hasMany`, `morphMany` or through relation they also honour the constraints written into it (`->where('paid', true)`) and a custom local key on `hasOne` / `morphOne`. On an Eloquent one-of-many relation (`latestOfMany()`, `ofMany()`) they cover every related row of the parent: the constraints written into that relation do not apply to them.
- **`latestByTimestamp()` / `oldestByTimestamp()` break a timestamp tie on the primary key**, as Eloquent's `latestOfMany()` does, so every database picks the same record for the card and its writes: with equal timestamps, latest now shows the highest id and oldest the lowest. The related model's `created_at` / `updated_at` are qualified with its table, so the default column works over a through relation that shares it; another column is used as given (qualify one that both tables have). +24 Pest.
- **`BelongsToMany` and `MorphToMany` ignored `hideSoftDeleteToggle()`, `hideRestoreAction()` and `hideForceDeleteAction()`.** Their panels passed only the search, per-page, create, edit and delete flags to the shared shell, so the trashed filter stayed whatever the field said. They now honour all three.
- **Republishing the assets deleted custom themes.** `martis:theme` writes the theme source to `resources/css/martis/<name>.css` and a copy to `public/vendor/martis/themes/<name>.css`, the file the panel loads. Since v1.8.8 `martis:publish-assets` wipes `public/vendor/martis/` before copying the package assets, and `martis:vendor-publish --assets` and `martis:install` run it, so every asset publish (each upgrade requires one) deleted the copy: the theme stylesheet returned 404 and the panel fell back to the default tokens with no error, and `martis:theme:diff` failed with "Consumer theme not found". After the package assets, `martis:publish-assets` now publishes every `.css` file directly inside `resources/css/martis/` to `public/vendor/martis/themes/`, and skips with a warning a file whose name the panel would not load, so the published themes always match their sources and a deploy that publishes restores them. +18 Pest. See [Theming → Theme files](docs/theming.md#theme-files).
- **A publish no longer destroys what it cannot write back, and stops instead of taking the panel's theme away.** `martis:theme` has always written both files, so a 1.x theme usually has its edits in the published copy and the untouched scaffold in the source. The first fix replaced such a copy without a word, deleted with exit 0 a published theme whose source was gone (also when `martis.theme.name` pointed at it), overwrote an edited copy under `--no-wipe`, which 1.x kept, and stopped after the wipe with a raw `ErrorException` on a broken symlink or unreadable file in `resources/css/martis/`. Before it deletes or overwrites anything, `martis:publish-assets` now stops with exit code 1, changing nothing, when the run would take away the theme `martis.theme.name` names (its source is skipped or cannot be read, or it has no source and the run would remove its published copy, a file or a symlink), when it would remove the published copy of a source it cannot read, or when `resources/css/martis/` cannot be listed (with its own message about the directory's permissions); `martis:install` stops with it. A source it cannot read that nothing depends on is skipped with a warning. Otherwise it backs up every file under `public/vendor/martis/themes/` it cannot write back (a copy edited in place, a theme without a source, a font or a hidden file next to a theme) to `storage/app/martis/theme-backups/<date>-<time>/`, under its path in the app, and warns with the backup's path and what to do; a failed backup stops the run before anything is deleted, and no warning announces a removal before every backup is made. A file already backed up with the same content is not copied again. A run that makes a new backup deletes the old runs beyond 10, keeping the oldest (it holds what the first publish after the upgrade took away) and the 9 newest; it lists each run it deletes, and a run it cannot delete is a warning. A copy the command published itself is replaced without a backup while its source exists, so the edit-and-publish loop stays quiet: the sha1 of each copy is recorded next to the copies, in `public/vendor/martis/themes/.published.json` (hashes only, no theme names), so a read-only `storage/` does not turn the next source change into a failed backup. Each entry is the sha1 of the theme name joined to the sha1 of the copy, so a copy of one theme placed over another's is backed up, not taken for a copy the publish wrote. `.gitkeep`, `.gitignore` and `.keep` there are never reported, backed up or removed, and a full publish puts them back after the wipe. Symlinks are never followed or written through: a link to a theme is replaced with a copy, any other link is removed and its target left alone. A `public/vendor/martis/themes/` that is itself a symlink (a Deployer or Envoyer shared directory) is checked and backed up like a directory, then replaced by a real one holding a copy of the target's files, with a warning, and nothing is ever written into the target: in 1.x the wipe removed only the link, which left the target alone but took the theme away from the panel, and `martis:theme` wrote its copy through the link into the target. The same goes for `public/vendor/martis/` itself as a symlink: since v1.8.8 the wipe emptied the target through the link, deleting any other file kept there, and the assets were copied into it. The link is replaced before any backup, so a link that cannot be replaced stops the run, exit 1, before any removal is announced. On a filesystem that ignores case, a published `Brand.css`, a file or a symlink, is the copy of the source `brand.css`: it is no longer removed right after the publish wrote to it, and the warnings name `brand.css`. A hidden file is no longer told to move to a hidden theme source. The theme sources are listed without Symfony Finder, whose `files()` drops broken symlinks since 7.4.19. `martis:theme` backs up the source it overwrites (after the edit-and-publish loop it holds the edits) and a published copy it cannot write back, trusting the record only while the source exists. The publish warns when `martis.theme.name` names a theme without a source that the run keeps, a name the panel ignores, or a name that differs from its source only in case, and skips with a warning a source the panel could not load (a `.CSS` extension, an invalid name). `martis:theme:diff` fails on a source it cannot read or a name the panel does not load, and warns when the published copy is missing or differs from the source. **Visible to 1.x users:** a deploy that shares `public/vendor/martis/themes/` between releases gets a real directory after the first publish (drop it from the shared list), and the old backup runs a publish deletes are listed in its output. +83 Pest. See [Theming → Theme files](docs/theming.md#theme-files).
- **"extension load exceeded 5000 ms" was logged for extensions that loaded fine.** The loader raced every extension import against a 5 s timer but never cleared the timer, so five seconds after each page load the console warned `[martis] extension load exceeded 5000 ms; mounting without it` for every bundle in `MARTIS_EXTENSIONS`, the message the docs give as the sign of a hung extension. The timer is cleared when the import settles. The loader moved to `resources/js/lib/extensionLoader.ts` and its test now exercises the real function instead of a copy of its logic (+2 Vitest).
- **Vitest collected the tests of nested worktrees.** Its default include globs the whole package with dotfiles allowed, so every git worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` doubled the local test count and mixed source trees through the `@` alias. `test.include` is `resources/js/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}` now.
- **`.gitignore` ignored nested source directories.** The unanchored `vendor/`, `coverage/` and `internal/` also ignored any directory of that name at any depth (case-insensitively on macOS); they are anchored to the repository root now. `node_modules` (no trailing slash) also ignores a symlinked `node_modules`, the usual way a git worktree reuses the main checkout's dependencies.
- **Repo-only files shipped in the Composer dist.** `.tooling/`, `phpstan-baseline.neon`, `AGENTS.md` and the tracked `.phpunit.result.cache` are `export-ignore` now, and `.phpunit.result.cache` (already in `.gitignore`) is no longer tracked.
- **Two native `title=` tooltips left.** The top-nav search button and the OfMany aggregate tile on a `HasOne` card still used the browser tooltip, which ignores the theme and competes with the global one. Both use `data-pr-tooltip` now, like the rest of the SPA.
- **`.tooling/pre-tag.sh` now checks the test count.** It had drifted from the workspace `pre-tag-check.sh`: a landing `TESTS_PASSING` that disagreed with the README total, or a landing component with a hardcoded count, passed the package copy. Both scripts run the same four checks again.
- **The Topbar ignored `profile.resource`.** `/api/auth/user` and the login response took the avatar from the default `ProfileResource`, while the profile page used the class `profile.resource` names, so with a custom resource the Topbar could show another avatar than the profile page. Both now resolve the same class, bound to `Martis\Contracts\ProfileResourceContract`; a resource whose `toArray()` has no `avatar_url` gives the Topbar none. The docs' custom resource example overrode a `fields()` method the resource does not have; it now shows the three methods of the contract. +8 Pest (`ProfileResourceConfigTest`; the `/api/auth/user` and login cases fail on the previous controller).
- **The Topbar kept the old name after an account update.** The profile page updated only its own state, so the Topbar showed the previous name until the next page load. It now passes the saved profile to the Topbar, on load and after the update. The Account section also reports the profile the server saved (the `PATCH /api/profile` response) instead of the form values, so a resource that drops or normalises a key shows what was saved. +3 Vitest.
- **The `martis:roles` `BulkAssignRole` stub builds its role picker with `pluck('name', 'id')`**, the order `options()` now reads. +1 Pest.
- **A `Select` option whose value is the empty string can be picked and shows as selected.** In v1.39.1 PrimeReact read the empty value as no value: picking the option handed the form the whole option object instead of `''`, a stored `''` showed the placeholder with no clear icon, and index and detail showed the dash instead of its label. The option now picks `''`, shows its label in the form, on index and on detail (`displayUsingValues()` keeps the dash), and a search in the control never matches the placeholder value that stands in for `''`. A `''` option returned by `searchOptionsUsing()` can be picked too and keeps its label in the form once picked, as does any option picked from the search results; index and detail read labels from `options()`, so list the option there too for them to show its label. +9 Vitest.

- **A relationship panel offered the actions a record's policy denies.** Every record a panel lists carries the related resource's policy answers under `_authorization`, which the resource index and the detail page already honour, but the `HasMany` / `HasManyThrough` / `MorphMany` / `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` panels and the `HasOne` / `MorphOne` cards showed View, Edit, Delete, Restore and Force delete by the field's flags alone, so a user the policy denies saw an action that then failed with a 403. They now leave out, per record, the ones its policy denies, and the id column links to the record only when `authorizedToView` allows it; a record without `_authorization` keeps them. +20 Vitest.
- **An upgrade kept serving the previous version's cached schema.** The Martis cache keys carried a per-layer counter and no package version, and the `schema` layer has no expiry, so after `composer update` a resource kept serving the fields, flags and actions of the previous version until `martis:cache:clear` or `cache:clear` ran, which neither the upgrade steps nor `martis:install` / `martis:publish-assets` do. Every key now carries the `martis/martis` version Composer installed (`martis:cache:{type}@{installed}:v{N}:…`; a dev version (`dev-main`, `2.x-dev`), whose name survives `composer update`, also carries its commit reference, `dev-main@0123456789ab`), so an upgrade rebuilds every layer (`metrics`, `navigation`, `dashboards`, `schema` and custom ones). A path repository keeps the version of its last `composer install` / `update`, so there clear the caches after pulling changes. +13 Pest. See [Cache → Invalidation](docs/cache.md#invalidation).

### Docs

- The troubleshooting guide told you to refresh a theme's published file by running `martis:theme` again, which asks before it overwrites the source (and refuses in a non-interactive run), then replaces your theme with the scaffold. It now says to run `martis:publish-assets --themes-only`, and its note on the legacy `vendor:publish --tag=martis-assets` says that path never publishes themes. The `theme.name` row of the configuration reference describes the key the panel reads.
- New [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md) page. The `Select` and `MultiSelect` references, the closure-aware setters and the actions, quick start, grid layout and panels pages use Nova's order, and the `pluck('name', 'id')` examples now store the id as they meant to. The filters page notes that filter options keep `[label => value]`, as in Nova; the REST overview documents `group` on the options payload.

Documentation and code comments that disagreed with the code:

- **Install flags.** `--with-profile` and `--with-2fa` are independent switches (the docs said `--with-profile` also publishes the 2FA migration and called `--with-2fa` a subset). `--force` also republishes `lang/vendor/martis` and rewrites the Martis migrations, and publishes no generator stubs (the option help said it did). The installer always ends with `migrate --force`, and without a TTY it writes disabled features to `.env`. The install options table lists `--no-profile`, `--no-2fa`, `--with-sessions` and `--no-sessions`.
- **Installation guide.** Core migrations list, the publish tags table (four tags were missing; `martis-migrations` bundles four stubs; no tag publishes the notifications table or the morph id conversions), the 2FA migration file name, the command list (34 commands, not 28), `martis:vendor-publish` and `martis:stubs` descriptions, npm instead of pnpm.
- **Default locale.** The panel language for users without a saved preference is `MARTIS_DEFAULT_LOCALE`, not `APP_LOCALE` / `MARTIS_LOCALE` (installation guide, configuration, i18n, troubleshooting, the config comment).
- **i18n.** A new locale's Martis strings go in `lang/vendor/martis/<locale>/`; `app_namespaces` merge per key; the translations endpoint converts `:name` placeholders to `{{name}}`.
- **Troubleshooting.** Metrics are cached by default through the `metrics` layer; `MARTIS_EXTENSIONS` has an empty config default; registry keys are colon-separated; new nested config keys need adding by hand (`mergeConfigFrom()` is shallow).
- **Authorization.** The "At a glance" defaults column (a policy without the method denies `view` / `create` / `update` / `delete`); Resource checks call the policy directly, so `Gate::before()`, the denial audit and the per-request Gate cache do not apply to them, and the per-request cache only records results.
- **Overrides and authentication.** The auth page keys are `auth:login`, `auth:register`, … (not `auth:*-page`); an auth override is built with `npm run build:extensions` in the app root.
- **Tool boot patterns.** `Schedule` is bound on every boot since Laravel 11, so the `app()->bound(Schedule::class)` guard never short-circuits; the pattern registers through `afterResolving()` like Laravel's `withSchedule()`.
- Smaller fixes: `loader.md` (`indexQuery()` signature), `cache.md` (the cache service is scoped), `customizing-generators.md` (`martis:roles`, the full stub list, the extension scaffold is not override-aware), `components.md` (the tooltip provider), `AGENTS.md` (asset republish command, a missing read-first file), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (pre-tag checks, smoke recipe), README (install flags, `--force`, npm), and code comments in `InstallCommand`, `ComponentMakeCommand`, `Sidebar.tsx`, `test-setup.ts`, `scripts/test.sh`, `vite.config.ts` and `phpstan.neon`.
- The relationships guide and the fields reference describe the Through fields as they now work, with an upgrade section for 1.x apps, and the per-record policy check on the panel actions. The code comments on `HasOneThrough` and `HasManyThrough` no longer say `->canCreate(true)` re-enables writes.
- The differentials page named four relationship hide flags that do not exist (`hidePerPage`, `hideTrashedFilter`, `hidePagination`, `hideDetachAction`), called two of them in its example and said the backend ignores hidden controls; it now lists the nine real flags and says they only change the panel.

### Internal

- **The `martis:mcp-serve` transport tests waited on fixed clocks.** The stdio test gave the subprocess 5s to finish, the readiness gate 8s, the SIGTERM check 10s, the HTTP calls 2-5s, and the `0.0.0.0` warnings were read after a 200ms sleep, so on a loaded machine (parallel suites, the Docker wrapper) the file failed whole or in part while CI passed. Every wait now ends when its condition holds (the port answers, the marker is logged, the process exits) under one ceiling, `MARTIS_TEST_PROCESS_TIMEOUT` (30s by default), and a failure says why: how long it waited, whether the process was running or its exit code, and its stderr and stdout. The stdio server is tracked for the forced cleanup too, and a finished server is stopped with SIGTERM under the same ceiling (a 2s stop escalated to SIGKILL, which leaves the skeleton's `.env`).
- **`CONTRIBUTING.md` says to build `public/` from an installed `node_modules`.** A build in a git worktree whose `node_modules` was a symlink to the main checkout wrote `../../../node_modules/...` keys into `public/manifest.json`, which the CI build check reports as a difference while a local rebuild in the same worktree shows none.
- **`profile.test.tsx` mocked `useAuth` without `updateUser`.** The profile page's load threw on the missing function and fell back to the auth user, so the page tests never ran the path that uses the loaded profile. The mock returns one stable spy now.
- **A local `vendor/bin/pest` no longer reads a `.env` left in the testbench skeleton.** The suite runs in the testbench skeleton under `vendor/`, and `Orchestra\Testbench\TestCase` loads the skeleton's `.env` when there is one. `vendor/bin/testbench`, which `McpServeCommandTransportTest` spawns, puts a `.env` in the skeleton while it runs (a copy of the package root's `.env`, `.env.example` or `.env.dist` when there is one, otherwise of the skeleton's `.env.example`) and deletes it only on a clean exit, so a subprocess killed with SIGKILL, or sent SIGTERM before it registers its signal handlers, left the copy behind. From then on its `CACHE_STORE=database` and `SESSION_DRIVER=cookie` failed about 1050 tests on every local run (`no such table: cache`, `Attempt to read property "cookies" on null`) while CI, which installs a fresh `vendor/`, stayed green on the same commit. PHP 8.5 and the locked Laravel 13.7.0 were not involved: with a fresh `vendor/` both pass. `tests/TestCase.php` sets `$loadEnvironmentVariables = false`, so the tests run on the skeleton's config defaults locally as in CI, and `McpServeCommandTransportTest` removes a `.env` its subprocess leaves. +1 Pest (`TestCaseEnvironmentTest`, which fails on the old `TestCase`). A stale `vendor/orchestra/testbench-core/laravel/.env` can be deleted; the suite ignores it now. The copy also broke `--parallel` from a clean skeleton: while the MCP subprocess ran, the other workers loaded it and failed about 830 tests, whose results exhausted the paratest parent at the 128M CLI memory limit.
- **Every test process runs in its own copy of the testbench skeleton, so the suite never writes under `vendor/`.** The workers shared the skeleton under `vendor/` that the suite writes into (`martis:install` publishes assets, config and migrations; the generators write `app/Martis` classes), so one worker read the files another was writing or deleting: 24 to 27 failures a run ("Martis frontend assets did not publish successfully", `Failed opening required …/config/…`, `strict_types declaration must be the very first statement`) and, now and then, a crashed worker. `TestCase::applicationBasePath()` copies the skeleton, on a worker's first test (paratest sets `TEST_TOKEN` in each worker), to a temporary directory named after the worker's process, and removes it when that process exits; a copy whose process could not clean up (Ctrl+C, SIGKILL) goes when the next copy is made, claimed first with an atomic rename so that, when every worker of the next run finds the same orphans on its first test, exactly one deletes each (they used to race, and a worker that lost threw from `applicationBasePath()` and failed every test it ran: 93 to 107 failures after a Ctrl+C), and a process started from a worker gets a copy of its own instead of removing the worker's. The copy keeps each file's modification time (Blade compares a compiled view's time with its source's, so copies stamped "now" served stale compiled views in parallel), and a failed copy throws, naming the path. `McpServeCommandTransportTest` runs `vendor/bin/testbench` in the same copy (`APP_BASE_PATH`). A sequential run gets a copy too (token `seq`, about 12 ms to make from a clean skeleton): the skeleton under `vendor/` is never written, where a sequential run used to leave some 1570 entries in it every time (published assets, translations, config, migrations, `app/Martis` classes, a rewritten `bootstrap/providers.php`, a deleted `app/Providers/.gitkeep`, a `laravel.log` growing about 590 KB a run), and that state (the leftover `.env` first) is what broke local runs. Checked with a fingerprint of the skeleton (files, modification times, links): identical before and after a full sequential run, three parallel runs and `scripts/test.sh`. +1 Pest, which drives the worker branch in subprocesses since CI runs sequentially: a killed worker's copy swept, the modification times kept, a child process's copy apart from the worker's; and +1 where four workers start at once over three orphans. Three consecutive parallel runs: 0 failed.
- **A local `vendor/bin/pest` runs under a stock `php.ini`, and `scripts/test.sh` pins nothing CI does not.** One process runs the whole suite, which peaks at about 170 MB (83 MB of it is loading the tests), while a stock `php.ini` allows 128M: a plain local run died part-way (`Allowed memory size of 134217728 bytes exhausted`), and CI passed because setup-php runs PHP with no memory limit. `phpunit.xml` now bootstraps through `tests/bootstrap.php`, which raises a limit below 1G to 1G and keeps a higher one or none (CI's `-1`, or a `-d memory_limit=…` on the command line). `scripts/test.sh` drops its `-d memory_limit=1G` and its `CACHE_STORE=array` / `SESSION_DRIVER=array` pins: the "cookies on null" errors the pins hid came from the `.env` left in the testbench skeleton the container mounts, which the suite now ignores.
- **`tests/TestCase.php` no longer loads Laravel's migrations before `RefreshDatabase`.** `migrate:fresh` runs on an empty folder (`tests/migrations-empty`, so it never runs the migrations `martis:install` publishes into the skeleton) and dropped their tables before every test, in CI too; the `users` columns `afterRefreshingDatabase()` added never ran either. Both are gone, and the comments now describe what the suite does: each test starts from an empty in-memory database (`migrations` and `martis_cache_state`) and creates the tables it needs. No test changes outcome.
- **Tests that change their skeleton copy's own files put them back for the tests after them; the suite pins the drivers it assumes and cleans up only its own temporary files.** `Martis\Tests\Support\SkeletonSnapshot` captures skeleton paths (contents and modification times, directories, symlinks as links) and restores them exactly. `ConsoleCommandsTest` restores `bootstrap/providers.php` and the host `MartisServiceProvider` stub (its cleanup rewrote the one and a test replaced it wholesale), `RolesScaffoldCommandTest` restores `app/Models`, `app/Providers`, `app/Policies`, `app/Martis/Resources` and `database/seeders` (its hooks deleted them, `app/Providers` included), `InvitationsScaffoldCommandTest` restores `app/Martis/Resources`, `app/Policies`, `app/Notifications`, `app/Providers` and `database/migrations` (the same hooks), and `McpServeCommandTransportTest` restores the skeleton's `vendor` link, which a killed `vendor/bin/testbench` left behind; each takes its snapshot before its first test and restores it after its last. Within a copy, the artifacts `martis:install` and the generators publish are still removed after each test that makes them, as before, and the copy with whatever is left goes when the process exits. `SsoMakeCommandTest` puts `.env`, `.env.example`, `config/martis.php`, `config/permission.php` and `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` back byte for byte and deletes the migrations the command published: it stripped only the lines the command added, so the skeleton's `.env.example` grew a few bytes a run, and the azure block written into `config/martis.php` reached later tests. Its listener test, skipped because the skeleton has no `AppServiceProvider`, now writes the one a fresh Laravel app has and runs. Its hyphenated-provider test read a `config/martis.php` it never published and passed only when an earlier test file had left one behind; it now publishes one first, like its siblings, and the file passes on its own. `ConsoleCommandsTest` no longer rewrites the skeleton's `.env`: `martis:install` writes no environment file while unit tests run, now a test. `TestCase` pins `app.debug` (false), `cache.default` and `session.driver` (`array`) and `queue.default` (`sync`), the skeleton defaults, since a variable exported in the shell still reaches the config. `PublishAssetsCommandTest` removes the temporary directories it created, not every `martis-pub-*` one (another suite's too), and `MigrationStubsUserKeyTest` and `McpServeCommandTest` no longer leave an empty `tempnam()` placeholder behind on every run. +2 Pest, and one test no longer skipped.
- **`Select` and `MultiSelect` read the stored value once per `resolve()`.** `Field::resolve()` now hands the value it reads to a protected hook, `inspectResolvedValue()` (a no-op by default), before `resolveUsing()` runs. The option-order check lives there, so `Select` no longer overrides `resolve()` and `MultiSelect` only decodes the result when there is no `resolveUsing()`: an accessor or a `computed()` callback runs once per read, as on v1.39.1, and a future change to `Field::resolve()` reaches both fields. +10 Pest: parity coverage (a plain attribute, `resolveUsing()` and `computed()`, each compared against a plain `Field`) and read counts for `Select` and `MultiSelect`, with and without `resolveUsing()` and `computed()`.
- **Hardened the "shows the label of a picked remote-only option" Vitest test** to wait for `.p-dropdown-panel` to close before reading the closed control's label: while the panel is still in the DOM, the picked option's own row also carries the label text, so the assertion passed even against the pre-fix `SelectField.tsx` (`ab2c2fc55`). Confirmed red against that revision, green again on HEAD.

## [1.39.1] — 2026-09-24

### Fixed

- **A callable config key set to the name of an invokable class did nothing.** `brand.page_title`, `gates.plan_resolver`, `profile.avatar.url_resolver` and an SSO provider's `role_source_callable` and `role_callable` tested their value with `is_callable()`, which is false for a class name. The invokable class name, one of the two forms that survive `php artisan config:cache` and the one the config comments recommended, was therefore ignored: `page_title` rendered the class name as the tab title, `plan_resolver` resolved no plan and locked every `requirePlan()` entity for every user, `url_resolver` fell back to the disk URL, and the SSO callables read no roles (with `on_no_role_match => 'guest'` and `sync_roles`, the login then synced the user's local roles away). All five now resolve through `Martis\Support\ConfigCallable`: an invokable class name is built through the container (constructor injection works), and a `[Class::class, 'staticMethod']` array or any other PHP callable is called as it is. `page_title` still renders a string that names no invokable class as the title, and never calls a string that names a PHP function (`Mail`, `Date`, `Link`). +67 Pest, among them a round trip of the documented forms through `var_export()` and `require`, as `config:cache` does it. See [Configuration → Config keys that take a callable](docs/configuration.md#config-keys-that-take-a-callable).

### Changed

- **A callable config key set to something that is not a callable throws.** A misspelt class name, a class without `__invoke()`, an instance method in the `[Class::class, 'method']` form or Laravel's `Class@method` string throws an `InvalidArgumentException` naming the key and the reason, where the key used to fall back silently (the locked plan gate, the disk URL, no SSO roles). `null`, `false` and an empty string still leave the key unset. From `role_source_callable` the exception fails the SSO callback like any provider error: it is logged and the login page shows the callback error.

### Docs

- The soft-gates page recommended an invokable instance (`new PlanResolver`) and a closure set from a service provider's `boot()`, and its example put a closure in `config/martis.php`; `config:cache` fails on all three, since it boots every provider before it writes the cache. The soft-gates, configuration, SSO and authentication pages, the `config/martis.php` comments and the `martis:agents` primer now name the two forms that survive it, the name of an invokable class and a static-method array, and the configuration page lists the five keys with their arguments. The `config/martis.php` example for `role_source_callable` took an `SsoIdentity`; it is called with `(string $externalId, string $accessToken)`. The `gates` comments and the `requirePlan()` docblock called `requirePlan()` without a resolver a no-op; it locks every user from a tier `plan_rank` declares.

## [1.39.0] — 2026-09-23

### Security

- **`primereact` 10.9.9.** It fixes a prototype pollution in `ObjectUtils.mutateFieldData` (CVE-2026-15538, GHSA-vg36-rrrg-4wqv), reached through DataTable cell editing with a field path an attacker controls. Martis does not use cell editing; the bundled copy is updated anyway (from 10.9.7). Its theme is byte-identical to 10.9.7's.

### Changed

- **Every PrimeReact component now paints with the Martis tokens.** Martis bundled the precompiled `lara-dark-indigo` theme, which paints with literal colours and reads none of the PrimeReact variables `martis.css` mapped to the tokens; a component followed the `--martis-*` tokens only where `martis.css` overrode its selectors. What the overrides missed kept the stock indigo and dark surfaces, in light mode too: the calendar popup in dark mode (lara's blue-grey surfaces, an indigo selected day) and any `MultiSelect`, `OverlayPanel`, `Menu` or `Slider` a Tool page or an extension renders (dark panels on the light theme). A consumer theme reached none of them. The theme is now compiled from the lara SASS source (`resources/sass/primereact`, MIT) with every colour variable pointed at a token, so light and dark mode, the accent presets and consumer themes restyle every PrimeReact component. The PrimeReact variables (`--primary-color`, `--surface-ground`, `--surface-0` to `--surface-900`, …) mirror the tokens for code that reads them. +4 Vitest. See [Theming → PrimeReact components](docs/theming.md#primereact-components-v1390).
- **The PrimeReact overrides in `martis.css` read the tokens too.** The danger buttons kept `#ef4444` in light mode, where `--martis-danger` is `#DC2626`; the outlined-button hover and the login button glow were indigo; the light input switch, the dropdown clear icons and the light toast text used fixed colours. They follow the tokens now. 32 overrides that restated what the theme paints are gone, each proven to change no computed colour in either mode, and `<Badge severity="…">` matches the `.martis-badge-*` pills (the profile page's session and 2FA badges). A consumer stylesheet that restyled PrimeReact selectors only to reach the Martis colours can drop those rules. +6 Pest.

### Docs

- The installation guide and the components page, the header of the published `vite.extensions.config.ts` and the code comments on the runtime and the install command still said the `react-dom` shim carries `createPortal` and nothing else; they now name `flushSync` too, which it carries since v1.38.2.
- The theming guide describes the token-driven PrimeReact theme and rewrites "Some colors don't change", which described the `lara-dark-indigo` limits.

## [1.38.2] — 2026-09-23

### Fixed

- **An extension using `@tanstack/react-virtual` (or any library that imports `flushSync` from `react-dom`) no longer built.** The `react-dom` shim of v1.38.0 re-exported the runtime's `createPortal` only, so the build stopped with `"flushSync" is not exported by ".shims/react-dom.mjs"`; `@tanstack/react-virtual`, the usual list virtualiser, imports it at the top of its entry and calls it while scrolling. With v1.37.3 `react-dom` went to the React shim, where a consumer could add a `flushSync` of its own. The runtime now carries the host's `flushSync` next to `createPortal` (`window.Martis.runtime.flushSync`), the runtime shim and the `react-dom` shim export it (named and on the default export) and the declarations type it, so a synchronous flush runs on the host's React DOM like a portal does; any other `react-dom` name still fails the build and `tsc`. Republish the shims to take it (`php artisan vendor:publish --tag=martis-extension-shims --force`). The runtime contract, shim and type-check tests cover it. See [Installation → Refreshing the extension scaffold after an upgrade](docs/installation-guide.md#refreshing-the-extension-scaffold-after-an-upgrade).

## [1.38.1] — 2026-09-23

### Changed

- **With the docs MCP wired, the `martis:agents` primer no longer repeats what the MCP serves.** It kept the 41-row slug table meant for file reads although `martis_doc_list()` returns the same index as data, stated the "read the docs only through the MCP" rule four times, carried transport and runtime-knob subsections that labelled themselves the operator's concern, and its section 9 restated eight rules of earlier sections without their reasons. The MCP-wired primer now names `martis_doc_list()` for the page index, states the rule once with its one instruction (on `enabled: false`, a 401 or a connection error, stop and tell the operator; never fall back to the files), and leaves the transport and the knobs to the agent guidelines page; section 9 keeps the one rule stated nowhere else, and the vendor and host-code rules moved into section 1 with their reasons. About 15 KB down to about 10 KB, no rule dropped. The file-based primer keeps the slug table with the file paths. +4 Pest. See [Agent guidelines → Docs are read through the MCP](docs/agent-guidelines.md#docs-are-read-through-the-mcp).

### Fixed

- **The `martis:agents` primer stamped the installed package version, so a committed primer went stale on every upgrade.** It opened with "package version `vX.Y.Z`", read from `vendor/composer/installed.json` when the file was generated: a project that upgraded without regenerating it shipped a primer naming a version it did not run, and one that regenerated it lost its edits (next entry). The primer names no version now and points the agent at `composer.lock`. +2 Pest. See [Agent guidelines → Customising the primer](docs/agent-guidelines.md#customising-the-primer).
- **The `martis:agents` primer template could not be customised, and `--force` discarded every edit.** The agent guidelines page said to publish it with `martis:stubs`, but `martis:stubs` only published the top-level stubs and the command always read the package's template, so the documented post-upgrade step (`martis:agents --force`) rewrote the primer from it. The command now resolves `agents/AGENTS.md.stub` like every generator stub (the project's `stubs/martis/agents/AGENTS.md.stub` first), `martis:stubs` publishes it there, and a published copy keeps its placeholders and MCP blocks working; with none published the output is the package's, byte for byte. +3 Pest. See [Customising Generators](docs/customizing-generators.md#what-gets-published).

## [1.38.0] — 2026-09-23

### Security

- **The `belongs-to-many` pivot action route called whatever parent model method the URL named.** `ActionController::executePivot()` only checked that the parent model had a method called `{relationship}`, then called it: `POST /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/belongs-to-many/delete/actions/{action}` deleted the parent (and answered 500) for any user who could view it and see one pivot action of the resource, whatever `authorizedToDelete()` said, and `forceDelete()` or any other public model method without arguments was reachable the same way. A real relation the resource never declares ran the action on its records too. The pivot action routes now resolve `{relationship}` only to a relationship field the resource declares with the route's type (`BelongsToMany` on `belongs-to-many`, `MorphToMany` on `morph-to-many`, nested layouts included) and call the model method only then; any other name answers 404 without touching the model (`Http\Controllers\Concerns\ResolvesPivotActions`). The pivot action listing resolves the parent the same way (403 when it cannot be viewed, 404 for an undeclared relationship) instead of answering 200 for any id and name.
- **A field placed directly in a `Tab` ignored `canSee()`.** `Tab::filterForContext()` applied only the context rules to the fields it holds directly, unlike `Panel` and `Section`. On every resource laid out with a `TabGroup`, a hidden field was therefore listed in the schema, its value was sent on the index, detail and update form, and the create and the update validated it and wrote the value the request sent. It now applies `canSee()` like a `Panel`. +7 Pest.
- **A field hidden for a record by `canSeeForModel()` / `canSeeUsingPolicy()` could still be written, and several reads sent its value.** The callback only ran when the resource's own endpoints serialised a record (index, detail, update form values, replicate). The resource create and update, the inline create and the inline create and update of the `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` panels validated the field and wrote the value the request sent: a user who could not see a record's `salary` could set it with a `PUT`, and the update form, which sends every field it lists, emptied it on every save. The records of the relationship panels (and their inline responses), the lens rows and the peek card sent the value, a relationship field it hid still served its panel, and a pivot field's callback was never asked. Every write now decides the field on the record it writes (`Field::filterForModel()`), as Nova resolves the fields of the resource instance a request updates: a hidden field is neither validated nor written and the column keeps its value. A create decides on the new, unsaved model before any value of the request is written to it, as Nova resolves creation fields on a fresh model; grant on `! $model->exists` to let a create write the field. A pivot field decides on the pivot row (the attached row on read and pivot update, a new row on attach, which stores its `default()`). Every read leaves the value out, and a relationship field hidden for the parent record answers 404 on every endpoint of its panel. +39 Pest. See [Fields → Field authorization](docs/fields.md#field-authorization-cansee-and-canseeformodel).
- **The fields of an Action ignored `canSee()`, and `handle()` received every value the request sent.** The fields endpoints of resource and pivot actions listed a field the user cannot see, the run validated it (a required hidden field failed the run), and `handle()` received whatever the request sent for it, as for a readonly or computed field and the fields of a Repeater's rows. As Nova resolves an Action's fields, the modal now leaves out a hidden field, and a run validates and takes from the request only the fields the user may set: a hidden, readonly or computed field is not validated and `handle()` receives its `default()` or nothing. A Repeater's rows are new rows under the Repeater's row rules (`Repeater::protectNewRows()`). `dryRun()` and a queued job receive the same values; a key no field names (a custom component's extra values) still reaches `handle()` as sent. +9 Pest. See [Actions → Fields the request cannot set](docs/actions.md#fields-the-request-cannot-set).
- **The per-field form endpoints answered for a field the user cannot see.** The relatable options (form, Action, pivot action, pivot fields), the remote `Select` search (resource and Tool), the Slug check and the `dependsOn` sync found their field without checking `canSee()`, in a Repeater row too. A hidden field therefore listed the options its scope derives, reported its reserved slugs, reacted to the form and revealed that it exists. The shared lookup (`MartisController::findField()`) now skips a field the user cannot see: `canSee()`, and `canSeeForModel()` for the record the form writes (the edited record, the new model of a create, the pivot row). A Repeater the user cannot see declares no row, and every endpoint answers exactly as for an undeclared attribute (404 for a picker and the Slug check, 422 for the `Select` search and the sync). +16 Pest.
- **A request could set a readonly or computed field inside a Repeater row, change an immutable one or write one the user cannot see, and every row carried the values of the fields hidden from the user.** `readonly()` on a row field only reached the form, and `computed()`, `canSee()` and `immutable()` had no effect inside rows: every storage mode (JSON, HasMany, polymorphic, pivot) wrote each value a row sent, and the Repeatable serialised every field with its value in every row. A row now continues the stored row whose id it carries: a readonly, computed or hidden field keeps the stored value and a new row stores its `default()` (or nothing), an immutable field is written on a new row and kept on a stored one (the update forms render it read-only on the stored rows), and a field the user cannot see is left out of the schema, of every row a read gives (`_pivot` included), of the row templates and default rows, and of the row validation. Matching the rows also ends two identity bugs: HasMany and polymorphic children were deleted and created again on every save (they are now updated in place, by primary key when the Repeater has no unique field), and a JSON Repeater whose `uniqueField()` is not `id` gave its rows new ids on every save. A JSON Repeater stored through an `AsCollection` / `AsArrayObject` cast read no rows at all, and a Repeater with one row type and row templates had no way to add a blank row; both are fixed. +26 Pest, +8 Vitest. See [Repeater → Readonly, computed, hidden and immutable row fields](docs/repeater.md#readonly-computed-hidden-and-immutable-row-fields).
- **`canSee()` was ignored on the pivot fields of a `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany`, and the resource schema listed the fields the user cannot see.** A hidden pivot field was serialised in the relationship's schema, listed in `_pivot`, validated and written from the request by the attach and the pivot update; it is now left out of all of them and never takes its value from the request (the attach stores its `default()`, the pivot update leaves the column alone). The schema's `fields` list, unlike its contextual arrays, carried every field of `fields()`; it now leaves out the hidden ones. +9 Pest. See [Relationships → With Pivot Fields](docs/relationships.md#with-pivot-fields).
- **A `HasMany` Repeater row could write any column of its child record.** `Repeater::saveRelated()` copied every key of a row's `fields` onto the child model, so a request could set the foreign key (moving the row under another parent record), the primary key or any column the Repeatable does not declare. Only the attributes of the Repeatable's fields are written now. Covered by the Repeater validation tests below.
- **`attachAny{Model}` never stopped an attach.** `authorizedToAttachAny()` is the parent-level attach ability, but the `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` attach and attachable endpoints never asked it: a user the policy denied could list and attach records (one or several) whenever `attach{Model}` allowed them, which it does when undefined. Both now answer 403 when it denies; `attach{Model}` still decides per record when it allows, and the detach and the pivot update keep their own abilities. +8 Pest. See [Authorization](docs/authorization.md).
- **A pivot action ran for any user who could view the parent, and a failed run returned its exception message.** `runPivotAction()` checked `viewAny`, `view` on the parent, `canSee()` and `canRun()` (which allows by default), but not the policy a resource action goes through: a user with only `view` on the parent ran a `DestructiveAction`, or an action that writes the pivot rows, on every attached record and got a 200. A pivot action now also needs the parent policy's `runAction` ability (`runDestructiveAction` for a destructive action), each falling back to `update` / `delete` like `execute()` does, and a denied run answers 404 as the resource action does. A failed run answered with the exception's message, which can carry SQL, paths or data; it now answers the generic error and the message goes to the action event log. +14 Pest (with the lifecycle below). See [Actions → Pivot actions](docs/actions.md#pivot-actions).
- **A user who could not edit a record read what its update form derived from it.** The relatable options (`GET /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/relatable/{field}`) and the Slug check (`…/slug-check/{field}?id=`) read the update form of the record their id names, so a `relatableQueryUsing()` closure could scope the options to that record, but they bound any record the id named after the `viewAny` check alone: a user who could neither view nor edit record 2 listed the options its update form derived from it (its team, its owner), saw its update form's `reserved()` list, and told its slug apart, since the Slug check left that record out of the uniqueness probe. The record is now bound only when `authorizedToUpdate()` passes for it (the check the edit form itself needs); any other record is answered from the create form, exactly like an id that names no record, so the answer neither carries anything derived from the record nor reveals whether it exists, and the Slug check leaves only the record being edited out of the probe. +4 Pest. See [Relationships → Relation fields declared on one form only](docs/relationships.md#relation-fields-declared-on-one-form-only).

### Added

- **Order the items of the bundled "System" sidebar section.** The section was assembled in a fixed order (System-section resources in registration order, then System-section Tools in discovery order, then the Cache admin link) and a host could not change it: neither opt-in took a weight, and rebuilding the section in `Martis::mainMenu(...)` left a second "System" header holding the cache link. `ResourceContract` and `ToolContract` gain `systemSectionOrder(): int` (default `100` on `Resource` and `Tool`), `Tool::withSystemSection()` takes an optional `order:` (`withSystemSection(order: 10)`; `null` keeps the current weight), and the new `martis.cache.admin_ui_order` (default `1000`) weighs the Cache link. `NavigationController` sorts the merged entries by weight with a stable sort, so equal weights keep the natural order and a host that sets nothing gets exactly the v1.37.3 section. Per-entry authorisation, the collapse of an empty section and the per-user + locale navigation cache are unchanged; the ⌘K palette keeps its alphabetical listing with the "System" tag. A published config that sets the weight to `null` keeps the default. Custom `ResourceContract` / `ToolContract` implementations that do not extend `Resource` / `Tool` must add the method. +12 Pest. See [Menus → Order inside the System section](docs/menus.md#order-inside-the-system-section-v1380).
- **`KeyValue::disableDeletingRows()`** (Nova parity). `disableEditingKeys()` and `disableAddingRows()` present a fixed set of keys, but every row kept its live delete button, so an operator could drop a row of a "fixed" map and the save removed that key. The new flag (`isDeletingRowsDisabled()`, serialised as `deletingRowsDisabled`) removes the button and turns the delete handler into a no-op; without it the button keeps working exactly as before, including on fields that only disable adding and key editing. The header spacer of the delete column now follows the button (also on readonly fields), and the button carries an accessible name. +2 Pest, +4 Vitest. See [Fields → KeyValue](docs/fields.md#keyvalue).
- **`NestedParentProvider` on `@martis/runtime`.** A custom override (a drawer or detail override registered in the component registry) that renders relationship fields through `runtime.FieldDisplay` / `runtime.FieldInput` for a record the page URL does not name had no way to tell the panels which record they belong to, so they read the page's record: the provider the bundled drawers and cards use was internal. The runtime and the published extension shim (`stubs/extensions/runtime-shim.mjs.stub`) now export it, with its `NestedParent` type: `<NestedParentProvider value={{ resource, id }}>` names the record, and `id: null` states that it does not exist yet (a custom create form). An extension scaffolded earlier reads it off the shim's default export (`runtime.NestedParentProvider`) or copies the new export line into its `.shims/runtime.mjs`. +2 Vitest. See [Overrides → Naming the record of the relationship panels](docs/overrides.md#naming-the-record-of-the-relationship-panels-v1380).
- **`@martis/runtime` exposes the registries, hooks and helpers the docs offered to extensions.** Since v1.8.19 a consumer builds its extension with its own Vite, where only `@martis/runtime` and the aliased third-party modules resolve, yet the docs kept importing these from internal `@/...` paths. The runtime and the published shim (`stubs/extensions/runtime-shim.mjs.stub`) now export them by name: the registries `componentRegistry` (the instance also on `window.Martis.componentRegistry`), `iconRegistry` and `layoutRegistry`; the hooks `usePageTitle`, `useModalHistoryLock` (it shares its lock count with the drawers, so it has to be the host's), `OverridePropsProvider` / `useOverrideProps` / `useOverridePropsOptional`, `useUnsavedChangesGuard` and `useError`; the helpers `cssVar`, `accentColor`, `mutedTextColor`, `chartPalette`, `resolveColor`, `avatarColorForSeed`, `Sparkline`, `ClearButton` and `MartisLoader` (the registry-aware wrapper, so an extension renders the loader registered under `loader`); and `usePreferences`, `usePreferencesOptional`, `loadLocale`, `applyDocumentDirection` and `usePrefersReducedMotion`, with the types `LayoutProps`, `OverrideProps`, `MartisLoaderProps` and `MartisLoaderConfig`. An extension scaffolded earlier refreshes its shim to import them by name: see [Installation → Refreshing the extension scaffold after an upgrade](docs/installation-guide.md#refreshing-the-extension-scaffold-after-an-upgrade). The main chunk grows by about 3 KB: the unsaved-changes guard, until now a lazy chunk, ships with it. +3 Vitest (`useError`, public now, had no test).
- **Per-resource page layouts: `layoutRegistry.register()` takes effect.** The override system documented `layoutRegistry.register('users', UsersLayout)` as the way to give one resource its own layout, but nothing in the SPA read the registry (only a test, deleted in April, called its `resolve()`), so a registered layout never rendered. A pathless route (`components/ResourceLayout.tsx`) now sits above the five resource routes (index, lens, create, detail, update) and renders the page inside the layout registered for the resource in the URL, within the shell, so the sidebar and topbar stay; a resource with no registered layout renders its pages as before, and the child paths and crumbs are unchanged. Extensions register through `@martis/runtime`. +3 Vitest. See [Overrides → Layout Overrides](docs/overrides.md#2-layout-overrides).

### Changed

- **An edit form mounts its fields once the record has filled it, so a stored slug stays put.** The update page and the update drawer rendered the fields one render before they seeded the form from the record, so every input mounted with an empty value and received the stored one on the next render. `Slug` decides when it mounts whether the slug was set by hand, so on an existing record it took the stored slug for an empty one and rewrote it from the source field as soon as that changed: renaming a record silently changed its URL on save. Both surfaces now keep their loading state until the record has seeded the form, so every input, a custom one included, starts from the stored value. On an edit form a stored slug no longer follows its source: edit it directly, or clear it (a `nullable()` slug shows a clear button) to regenerate it from the source and follow it again. A create form is unchanged. +4 Vitest (update page, update drawer), and the update-page characterization of the slug now pins the stored slug. See [Fields → Slug](docs/fields.md#slug) and [Overrides → Input Component](docs/overrides.md#input-component).
- **`BelongsToMany` and `MorphToMany` never render on a create form, like Nova.** A pivot row needs the key of the record it attaches to, which a create form does not have yet, and Nova drops its `ListableField`s from every creation form. Both fields were hidden on create by default (v1.8.4), but `showOnCreating()` or `onlyOnForms()` put them back on the create page, the create drawer and the inline-create modal (`fieldsForCreate`, `fieldsForInlineCreate` and `inline-create-schema`), where the panel could only read another record or none (see Fixed). The schema now keeps them off every create form whatever visibility the resource asks for, and serialises `showOnCreate: false` (`Fields\Concerns\StaysOffCreateForms`); the detail page and the update form keep them. Attach once the record exists, or use `Tag` to pick related records while creating. **Breaking** for a resource that relied on `showOnCreating()` to render a custom create-context component for one of these fields. +13 Pest. See [Fields → BelongsToMany](docs/fields.md#belongstomany).
- **Replicate mounts the form once the copy has filled it, and a `Slug` on a create form follows its source from the first change, like Nova's.** The replicate page mounted the fields as soon as the copy arrived and filled the form one render later, so an input that reads its value when it mounts showed nothing of the copy, and a `Slug` replaced the copied slug with one made from the copied title. The page now keeps its loading skeleton until the copy has filled the form, as Nova's Replicate view does; the error page still shows, and a copy without `values` opens an empty form. A `Slug` on a create form (the create page, the create drawer, the inline-create and action modals, Tool forms) took a slug it opened with for one typed by hand, and filled an empty slug from a source that already had text. It now keeps the slug it opens with until the source changes, then follows it until edited by hand. Edit forms still keep a stored slug. Same on v1.37.3. +11 Vitest. See [Fields → Slug](docs/fields.md#slug) and [Fields → Resource Replication](docs/fields.md#resource-replication).
- **Pivot actions run like resource actions, as in Nova.** A pivot action always ran `handle()` inline: `ShouldQueue` ran it synchronously, `withDryRun()` was ignored, the `then()` callback never ran and no action event was written, which the docs stated as a limitation. A dry run (`dryRun: true`) now answers the action's `dryRun()` preview without running it; a `ShouldQueue` action is dispatched as `Actions\Jobs\ExecutePivotAction`, which reloads the rows through the parent's relationship with the declared pivot columns so `handle()` receives each record with its pivot row, as the inline run does; and every run writes one action event per row with Nova's mapping (the parent as `actionable`, the related record as `target`, the pivot row as `model`, and the pivot columns the run changed as `original` / `changes`), `queued` rows settling to `completed` or `failed` when the job ends (`Actions\PivotActionEventLog`). `withoutActionEvents()` and `martis.action_events.enabled` switch the log off, as on a resource action. See [Actions → Pivot actions](docs/actions.md#pivot-actions).

### Fixed

- **A `KeyValue` edit form showed none of the stored rows.** The input copied its value into local state when it mounted and never read the prop again, but both edit surfaces (the update page and the update drawer) mounted the fields before they seeded the form from the record, so the rows arrived one render too late: an existing map rendered empty, and adding a row replaced the whole stored map with that single row on save. The input now adopts a value that did not come from its own `onChange` (the record seeding the form, a form reset) and keeps its rows when the form hands back what it just emitted, the same late-value handling `Sparkline` and multiple `BelongsTo` already had. +3 Vitest (component, and the update page rendering the stored rows).
- **The `MorphTo` and `Tag` pickers listed "[object Object]" for every option** when the related resource shows its title attribute through a formatted index field (a `Stack`): relatable rows carry the index values, and only `BelongsTo` read the text out of such a value. The three pickers now share `lib/relatedRecordLabel.ts` (heading of a Stack, then the resolved `_title`, common attributes, `#id`). +4 Vitest.
