- **A plain `data-pr-tooltip` longer than one line spilled out of its bubble.** The global tooltip pinned plain text to one line (`white-space: nowrap`) inside a 300 px box with no `overflow` rule, so a sentence was painted past the bubble over the page; wrapping only existed behind the `data-pr-tooltip-html="true"` opt-in, which pushed consumers to send escaped plain text through the HTML sink. Both channels now wrap (`white-space: normal`, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, `max-width: min(360px, 100vw - 16px)`): a one-word label stays a single-line pill, a sentence wraps inside the bubble and a long unbroken token breaks inside it. Plain text is still rendered as text. The ref-based PrimeReact `<Tooltip>` (the one the docs recommend for JSX content) kept `white-space: nowrap` inside a 300 px `.p-tooltip-text` and spilled the same way; it now wraps like the global tooltip and keeps explicit line breaks (`white-space: pre-line`). +2 Vitest, +1 Pest. See [Fields → Tooltips](docs/fields.md#tooltips-martis-differential).
- **The tooltip bubble was never kept inside the viewport.** It was anchored with `left` at the trigger's centre and pulled back with `translate(-50%)`, so nothing compared it with the viewport (a trigger near the right edge put up to half the bubble off-screen) and layout shrink-to-fit it against the space between the trigger and the edge (the same text was 360 px wide mid-page and 220 px against the edge). The bubble is now laid out at the viewport origin, measured, and positioned with a transform computed by the new `lib/tooltipPlacement.ts`: it flips to the opposite side when the requested one has no room for its measured size (replacing the fixed 36 px clearance; a `left` / `right` bubble with room on neither side goes above or below), is clamped 8 px inside the viewport, and its arrow moves along the edge to keep pointing at the trigger. While open it follows the trigger through page and container scrolls (it used to stay where the trigger was) and closes once the trigger leaves the viewport. +13 Vitest (placement), +4 Vitest (component). See [Components → Long text and viewport edges](docs/components.md#long-text-and-viewport-edges).
- **A host that built the System section itself got a second "System" header.** The `Martis::mainMenu(...)` dedup dropped the System-section resources and Tools a host placed by hand, but the Cache admin link was appended unconditionally after the resolver, so the bundled section survived with that link alone. The link is now skipped when the resolved menu already holds an in-app link to `/system/cache` (trailing slash, query and fragment ignored; external links never match). Covered by the System-order tests above.
- **A structured field without an `array` rule still stored a non-JSON string.** v1.37.3 decodes JSON strings of structured fields (`Repeater`, `MultiSelect`, `BooleanGroup`, `KeyValue`, `Tag`, `MorphTo`, `Sparkline`) before validation and documented that a string which does not decode "fails validation instead of being stored", but that only held for fields declaring an `array` rule: otherwise the string reached `fill()`, which emptied a `Repeater`'s rows, cleared a `KeyValue` map or a `MultiSelect`, detached every `Tag` or stored the text in a `BooleanGroup` column. This is what a pre-1.37.3 bundle (assets not republished) or a hand-written API call sends. The new `Field::rejectsUnstructuredValue()` is `true` for a structured field the package fills itself and `false` where no data is at risk (a readonly or computed field, a `fillUsing()` callback that owns the shape, and `MorphTo`, whose `fill()` ignores anything that is not a target map); `DecodesStructuredValues::decodeStructuredValues()` returns the attributes of such fields whose value is neither a list or map nor a string that decodes to one (a non-JSON string on either path, and a number or a boolean a JSON request sends, such as `{"meta": 5, "labels": true}`, which the built-in fills also turned into an empty value), and the five resource controllers validate those with `array` on top of the field's rules, so the request fails with a 422 and the stored value is untouched on both request paths; `null` and the empty string still clear the field. `Repeater::fill()` ignored `fillUsing()` and `computed()` and always wrote the rows; it now runs the callback, and a computed Repeater writes nothing, like every other field. +16 Pest. See [Fields → Structured values and file uploads](docs/fields.md#structured-values-and-file-uploads).
- **A `MorphTo` changed on an edit form was never saved.** The update page and the update drawer reduced every `{ id, title }` value to its id, which is right for a `BelongsTo` but also caught the `MorphTo` map (`{ type, id, title, resourceType }`); the server cannot place a bare id and ignored it, so a new target picked on an edit form was silently dropped (create forms were fine). Both surfaces now build their payload with the shared `lib/updatePayload.ts`, which keeps a map carrying `resourceType`. On the server `MorphTo::fill()` now honours a submitted `type` only when it names one of the field's `types()` (model class or morph-map alias), as it already did for `resourceType`, instead of writing any class name it received. +5 Vitest (helper, and the update page submitting both shapes), +2 Pest. See [Fields → MorphTo](docs/fields.md#morphto).
- **The relationship write endpoints skipped field rules that the resource endpoints run.** On update, the `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` inline forms and the `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` pivot update meant to drop only the literal `required` (so a partial payload leaves the other fields alone) but filtered each field's rules down to strings, while `ResourceController` keeps every other rule. The pivot updates therefore skipped every rule object and closure (`Rule::unique()`, `Rule::in()`, a `ValidationRule`, a closure). The inline updates ran the filter through a typed callback: closures and objects implementing the legacy `Rule` contract (`Rule::enum()`, `Password::min()`) or stringifying to `required` (`Rule::requiredIf()` with a true condition) were skipped, `Stringable` builders (`Rule::unique()`, `Rule::in()`, `Rule::exists()`) only survived through PHP's string coercion, and a related resource declaring a `ValidationRule` instance answered every inline update with a 500 (`TypeError`), whatever the payload. No relationship endpoint passed a context to `buildRules()` either, so `creationRules()` never ran on an inline create or an attach and `updateRules()` never ran on an inline or pivot update. The seven write paths (the resource's own create and update included) now build their rules through one `BuildsFieldRules` trait: `creationRules()` apply on every create and attach, `updateRules()` on every update, and on update only the literal `required` string is dropped and `sometimes` leads the list, so every rule a field declares runs for a key the request sends and none runs for a key it omits. An update, inline create or attach that a skipped rule used to let through is now rejected with a 422. +61 Pest. See [Fields → What an update validates](docs/fields.md#what-an-update-validates).
- **An inline update overwrote the related record's `immutable()` fields.** `immutable()` makes a field writable on create and silently skipped on update, but only `ResourceController::fillFields()` skipped it: the `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` controllers fill through their own `fillFields()`, which had no such check, and `Field::fill()` only honours `readonly()`. A `Text::make('slug')->immutable()` that the resource's own PUT left untouched was therefore overwritten when the record was edited from its parent's detail page (`PUT …/has-many/{relationship}/{relatedId}`, `…/has-one/{relationship}`, `…/morph-many/{relationship}/{relatedId}` and `…/morph-one/{relationship}`). The four `fillFields()` now skip an immutable field once the record exists, exactly as the resource endpoint does: the request is accepted, the other fields are written and the column keeps its value, while the inline creates still write it. The pivot fields of a `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` are covered by the next entry. +10 Pest. See [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **The pivot endpoints wrote `readonly()` and `immutable()` pivot fields from the request.** The `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` attach and pivot update wrote the pivot row through Eloquent, never through `Field::fill()` (they do now, see the next entry), and copied every pivot value the request sent: a `readonly()` pivot field was writable through `POST …/belongs-to-many/{relationship}/attach` and `PUT …/belongs-to-many/{relationship}/{relatedId}/pivot` (and their `morph-to-many` twins), and the pivot update overwrote an `immutable()` one. Both controllers now validate and collect the pivot values through one `CollectsPivotData` trait, which replaces their four copies of that code. A readonly pivot field never takes its value from the request: the attach stores its `default()`, as it does for any pivot field the request omits (so a readonly field with a default stamps the row with a value the client cannot change), and the pivot update leaves the column alone. An immutable pivot field is written on attach and skipped on the pivot update, as on the resource endpoint. Every pivot field is still validated, and the update response's `pivot` lists only the values written. A pivot update with nothing left to write (an empty body, or only readonly and immutable values) now answers 200 and runs no query; it used to answer 500, because `updateExistingPivot()` compiled an `UPDATE` with an empty `SET` on a relation without `withTimestamps()` or `using()`. +24 Pest. See [Relationships → With Pivot Fields](docs/relationships.md#with-pivot-fields) and [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **Pivot fields were written without their `fill()`.** The attach (single and batch) and the pivot update of both many-to-many panels copied the raw request value into the pivot row, so a pivot field's `fillUsing()` never ran, a computed pivot field was written into a column that does not exist, a `MultiSelect` or `KeyValue` stored a PHP array Eloquent cannot bind, and a `Boolean` stored the string the multipart path sends. `CollectsPivotData` now fills a pivot model from `newPivot()` through each field's `fill()` and writes the attributes the fields set; for a custom pivot class (`using()`), whose casts `attach()` and `updateExistingPivot()` apply themselves, it passes the cast-level values so a structured value is encoded once. +7 Pest. See [Relationships → With Pivot Fields](docs/relationships.md#with-pivot-fields).
- **The edit forms rendered an `immutable()` field as an editable input.** The schema serialises `immutable`, but no form read it: the update page (also when a relation panel opens the record to edit it), the update drawer and the form that edits a pivot row showed an editable input, and the update dropped the new value silently (200, column unchanged). The update forms now render an immutable field read-only, as they render a `readonly()` field, and still submit its stored value: `useMartisForm` marks it `readonly` in `resolvedFields` when its `context` is `update` (so a Tool form bound to a record does the same), and the update drawer and the pivot edit form apply the same `lib/lockImmutableFields.ts`. The create page, the create drawer, the inline create and the attach keep the input editable, and a field inside a `Repeater` row is left alone, since the Repeater writes its rows as one value. `FieldDefinition` types the flag as `immutable?: boolean`. The `Slug` lock hint no longer ends with "after publish", a cause the package cannot know. The five inputs that ignored `readonly` (`Avatar`, `BooleanGroup`, `Repeater`, `File`, `Image`) are fixed below. +14 Vitest. See [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **A `Tag` edit form showed none of the stored tags, and adding one detached them all on save; several inputs kept the previous record after "Create & add another".** Like `KeyValue` above, the `Tag` input copied its value into local state when it mounted and never read the prop again, so on the edit surfaces the stored tags arrived one render too late: the field rendered empty, picking a tag submitted that tag alone, and `Tag::fill()` synced the relationship to it, detaching every stored tag. `Avatar` (an edit form showed neither the stored image nor its Remove button) and `Repeater` with `collapsedByDefault()` (the stored rows opened expanded) read their value at mount the same way. The same flaw showed when "Create & add another" cleared the form for the next record: `Tag` kept the previous record's tags and sent them with the next pick, `BelongsTo` kept showing the parent picked for the previous record while its value was empty (the next submit sent no key: silently empty when nullable, a 422 on a field that looked filled when required), and a slug typed by hand kept the next record's slug from following its title. These inputs now adopt a value that did not come from their own `onChange` (the record seeding the form, a cleared form) and keep their state when the form hands back what they just emitted; a `Repeater` starts the rows it receives that way collapsed, and a slug the form clears follows its source again. The `Avatar` file picker is re-armed after each pick, so the same file can be picked again after Remove or a cleared form. +22 Vitest (the inputs, the update page keeping the stored tags on save, "Create & add another" with a `Tag` and a `BelongsTo`).
- **A `Repeater` kept only the last of several row-field updates made before the form handed its rows back.** Every row field emits through the Repeater's `onChange`, and each update copied the rows of the Repeater's last render, so when several row fields emitted before the Repeater re-rendered, each update started from the same rows and only the last one reached the form. Every stored row whose `Slug` generates itself from its source while it mounts does exactly that on an edit form: two stored rows with empty slugs ended up as `[null, 'second']`, the first row's slug input stayed empty and the save sent it that way. Any row input that normalises its value while it mounts, a custom one included, lost its update the same way. Each update (a row field, add, duplicate, remove, reorder, bulk paste) now builds on the rows the Repeater emitted last, until the form hands it a new value, which it then builds on. Same on v1.37.3. +3 Vitest. See [Overrides → Input Component](docs/overrides.md#input-component).
- **A picker declared on a form only opened with no options.** `ResourceController::relatableOptions()` looked the relation field up in `fields()` alone, so a `BelongsTo`, `MorphTo` or `Tag` that a resource declares in `fieldsForCreate()` or `fieldsForUpdate()` answered `Field 'x' not found.` (404) and its picker listed nothing. The endpoint now reads the form the picker renders in: `fieldsForUpdate()`, on the resource bound to the record, when the `{id}` segment names one; otherwise `fieldsForCreate()` and then the inline-create modal's `fieldsForInlineCreate()`; `fields()` last. The form's own declaration wins over a differently configured one in `fields()` (its `relatableQueryUsing()` applies, and can read the record under edit), and only a `BelongsTo`, `MorphTo` or `Tag` matches the attribute, so a read-only `Text` that reuses it on a form does not hide the picker declared in `fields()`. The `viewAny` gates and the relatable query hooks are unchanged. One `MartisController::findFormField()` now serves every per-field form endpoint and replaces their three layout flatteners: the Slug check reads the form its `id` names before `fields()` (it read `fields()` first, so the form's own `reserved()` list lost), and the Slug check, the `dependsOn` sync and the server-side `Select` search find a field declared only in `fieldsForInlineCreate()` (they answered 404 or 422). +19 Pest. See [Relationships → Relation fields declared on one form only](docs/relationships.md#relation-fields-declared-on-one-form-only).
- **The pickers of an action modal asked the page's resource for their options, and a create form nested in another resource's page sent the page's record id.** `BelongsTo`, `MorphTo` and `Tag` filled whatever the input did not say from the page URL. The action modal renders an Action's fields with neither a resource nor a record, so its pickers asked the page's resource (and record) for the attribute: one only the Action declares answered 404 and the picker opened empty, and one the resource also declares listed the resource's options (its related resource, its `relatableQueryUsing()`) instead of the Action's. They now load from `GET /api/resources/{resource}/actions/{action}/relatable/{field}`, which finds the field in the Action's `fields()` and applies that declaration, with the resource the Action runs on as the source of the relatable hooks; it is gated like running the Action (`viewAny` on the resource, the Action's `canSee()`) and then on `viewAny` of the related resource. The modal hands the pickers the Action's base path through a new `actionEndpoint` input prop, which a custom Action component can pass to `FieldInput` as well. The pivot action modal of a `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` panel asks `/{resource}/{id}/{belongs-to-many|morph-to-many}/{relationship}/actions/{action}/relatable/{field}`, which finds the Action where the panel's fields endpoint finds it (the field's `actions()`, then the resource's `pivotAction()` ones) behind the same gates, with the parent resource as the source of the hooks. The inline-create modal passed its own resource but the page's record id, so on an edit page the form lookup above read the update form of whichever record of the modal's resource had that id, and a picker declared only on that resource's create forms answered 404 (a create drawer opened from a detail page did the same). A picker rendered with `context="create"` now sends `_` unless the host passes a `recordId`, and the page's record id only reaches an input scoped to the page's own resource. The three pickers share `lib/relatableEndpoint.ts`. +25 Pest, +23 Vitest. See [Relationships → Relation fields declared on one form only](docs/relationships.md#relation-fields-declared-on-one-form-only) and [Actions → Relation fields](docs/actions.md#relation-fields).
- **An edit form kept the first record when the route moved to another record.** The router reused the update page when the URL moved from one record's edit page to another's (a link in the form, back/forward between two edit pages, a `redirectAfterUpdate()` to another record's edit URL), so the second record never seeded the form: the page showed the first record's values, the edits the user had just agreed to discard included, and Save sent them to the second record's id. A record of another resource kept the first resource's form. The update drawer did the same when its host handed it another record without remounting it (Edit on another index row while the drawer is open, an action response that opens another record, the detail page moving to another record behind the open drawer), and it kept the previous record's validation errors and dirty baseline too. The update page now starts over for each record in the URL (keyed by resource and id), and the update drawer seeds its form again, errors and dirty baseline included, when its resource or record changes; in both, a fresh copy of the same record keeps the edits. Separately, with `confirmUnsavedChanges()` on, a `redirectAfterUpdate()` to the record's own edit page left the values just saved counted as unsaved, so leaving the page asked to discard them: every successful save now refreshes the guard's baseline, as "Save & continue editing" already did. Same on v1.37.3. +16 Vitest (update page, update drawer); `test-support/dataRouterNavigation.ts` lets a Vitest file navigate a data router under jsdom. See [Overrides → Override Props](docs/overrides.md#override-props).
- **A create form kept what was typed or prefilled for the previous target when the route moved to another one.** The router reused the create page when the URL moved to another resource's create page, another parent's nested create or another record to replicate (a `redirectAfterCreate()`, a menu link, back/forward between two create pages), so the second form started from what the user had typed for the first, even after agreeing to discard it, and sent it; a nested create kept showing the first parent in its locked `BelongsTo` (the server still attached the record to the parent in the URL), and a replicate kept the first source's values. The create page now starts over for each target, keyed by resource, `viaResource` / `viaResourceId` / `viaRelationship` and `fromResourceId`; a navigation that keeps the target keeps what was typed. Same on v1.37.3. +4 Vitest. See [Resources → Custom post-save destination](docs/resources.md#custom-post-save-destination--redirectaftercreate--redirectafterupdate).
- **Five inputs ignored `readonly()`, so a readonly field of those types (and an `immutable()` one on an update form) still took a change that the save dropped.** `Avatar`, `BooleanGroup` and `Repeater` never read `field.readonly`, and `File` / `Image` only disabled their hidden file input: the avatar picker and its Choose file / Remove buttons, the checkboxes of the group, the Repeater's add, remove, duplicate, reorder, bulk paste, row templates and row fields, and the remove buttons and drop zones of `File` / `Image` kept changing the value. `Field::fill()`, `File::fill()`, `Image::fill()` (inherited by `Avatar`) and `Repeater::fill()` skip a readonly field, and the update forms hand an `immutable()` field to its input as `readonly` (see above), so the save answered 200 with the column unchanged. These inputs now render a readonly field read-only: `Avatar` shows the stored image with the picker disabled and no buttons; `BooleanGroup` disables every checkbox; `Repeater` shows its rows (the collapse toggles still work) with read-only row fields and no control that adds, removes, duplicates, reorders or pastes rows, and drops a change that a custom row input emits anyway; `File` / `Image` keep their download links and previews, lose the remove and Change buttons, disable the picker and ignore a dropped file, with the drop zone in its `.martis-dropzone.is-readonly` state. An editable field works as before. Same on v1.37.3 for `readonly()`. +38 Vitest. See [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **A `MorphToMany` panel showed no pivot actions, and every render logged a 404.** The panel requested `/api/resources/{resource}/{id}/morph-to-many/{relationship}/actions` and posted to `.../actions/{action}`, but only the `belongs-to-many` pivot action routes existed. The `morph-to-many` routes now exist and share the `belongs-to-many` implementation: the listing, the fields endpoint below and the run, which loads the related models with the pivot columns the field declares (they were read only from a `BelongsToMany` field) and filters them by the qualified related key (a morph pivot with its own `id`, like a typical `taggables` table, made the bare `id` ambiguous: 500).
- **`BelongsToMany::actions()` and `MorphToMany::actions()` did nothing.** Both fields stored the closure and nothing read it, so a panel only ever listed the resource actions flagged `->pivotAction()`, which show on every many-to-many panel of the resource. The actions a field declares now show on that relationship's panel only, before the resource pivot actions (a field action wins over a resource pivot action with the same URI key); the closure receives the request, and an action declared there needs no `->pivotAction()` (`Fields\Concerns\HasPivotActions`). The pivot action modal read the action's fields from the resource action endpoint, which only knows the resource's own actions, so it now reads them from the new `GET /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/{belongs-to-many|morph-to-many}/{relationship}/actions/{action}/fields`, gated like the run. Both panels use one `PivotActionModal`.
- **Pivot actions with different `referToPivotAs()` labels opened every dropdown at once, and only the last dropdown's items could be picked.** The panel renders one dropdown per label but kept one open flag and one element ref for all of them, so a click opened every dropdown, and the mousedown on an item of any other dropdown counted as a click outside and closed them before the click landed. Each dropdown now opens on its own.
- **A file dropped on a readonly `Audio` field opened in the browser tab and left the form.** The input's `dragover` handler always cancels the event, so the browser lets a file be dropped on the field, but on a readonly field (a `readonly()` one, or an `immutable()` one on an update form, which the update forms hand to the input as `readonly`) its `drop` handler returned before cancelling the drop. The browser then ran its default action: it opened the dropped file in the tab, leaving the form and its unsaved changes. The drop is now cancelled first, as `File` / `Image` do, and a readonly field ignores the file; an editable field takes it as before. Same on v1.37.3 for `readonly()`. +4 Vitest. See [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **Relationship panels inside a `HasOne` / `MorphOne` card or a record drawer listed the related records of the page's record.** A `HasMany` (`HasManyThrough` included), `MorphMany`, `BelongsToMany` or `MorphToMany` panel took its parent from the page URL (`BelongsToMany` from the route params, the others by parsing `window.location`) and ignored the card that rendered it: on `/resources/team-members/2`, the panels of the `firstManagedProject` card (project 3) asked `/api/resources/team-members/2/has-many/tasks`, `.../morph-to-many/tags` (its pivot actions too) and `.../morph-many/comments` (404 and empty panels, or the page record's own rows when it declares the same relationship), and their Create buttons carried `viaResource=team-members&viaResourceId=2`. Only the `HasOne` / `MorphOne` cards read the enclosing card. The bundled record drawers (`DrawerDetail`, `DrawerUpdate`, `DrawerQuick`) named no record at all, so the detail drawer opened from a lens row asked `/api/resources/clients/lens/has-many/...` and a drawer an action opened over another record's page asked that record. Every relationship panel now resolves its parent through one hook, `useRelationParent()` (the enclosing card's or drawer's record, else the route's `:resource/:id`), and the three drawers provide the record they show; at the top level of a detail or edit page the panels read the same record as before. Same on v1.37.3. +30 Vitest. See [Relationships → Which record a panel belongs to](docs/relationships.md#which-record-a-panel-belongs-to).
- **A `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` panel on a create surface read the page's record, and a Replicate drawer attached to the record it copies.** Declared with `showOnCreating()`, the panel took its parent from the page URL: the create page names no record, so it asked `/api/resources/{resource}//belongs-to-many/{relationship}` (404; `morph-to-many` alike), and a create drawer or the inline-create modal opened over a record's page listed that record's related rows, with Attach, Detach and the pivot actions writing to it. A Replicate drawer on `/resources/projects/3` listed and attached to project 3, a create drawer an action opened on another record's page read that record, and an inline-create modal opened inside a record drawer read the drawer's record. Besides the schema change above, every create surface (the create page, `DrawerCreate`, `InlineCreateModal`) now states that its record does not exist yet (`NestedParentProvider` with `id: null`), and a pivot panel with no record renders nothing and asks nothing, so a form built by hand does not read another record either. +12 Vitest. See [Relationships → Which record a panel belongs to](docs/relationships.md#which-record-a-panel-belongs-to).
- **The documented `@martis/runtime` imports failed consumer extension builds.** A consumer build resolves `@martis/runtime` to the published `resources/js/martis-extensions/.shims/runtime.mjs`, which exports the runtime members by name and the runtime object as its default export, and has no `martisRuntime` export. The nine examples in `docs/overrides.md` (section 5.A) and `docs/tool-fields.md` imported `{ martisRuntime }`, so a copied example stopped the build with `"martisRuntime" is not exported by ".../.shims/runtime.mjs"`. They now import each name (`import { FieldInput } from '@martis/runtime'`), like the stubs the package ships. `Dropdown`, `MultiSelect` and `createPortal`, on the runtime since v1.29.0, had no named export in the shim and failed the same way; the shim now exports them. `martisRuntime.test.tsx` requires a shim export for every runtime member and for every name a doc imports from `@martis/runtime`. The shim is copied into the app once, so an extension scaffolded earlier refreshes it: see [Installation → Refreshing the extension scaffold after an upgrade](docs/installation-guide.md#refreshing-the-extension-scaffold-after-an-upgrade), which also lists the version that added each named export. +1 Vitest.
- **The legacy import paths of the extension Vite config never resolved.** `vite.extensions.config.ts` sends `@/contexts/*`, `@/lib/*`, `@/components/auth/*` and `@martis/martis/*` to the runtime shim so that override files published before v1.10.0 keep building, but each pattern matched only the start of the path, and the alias replaces only what it matches: `@/lib/api` became `.shims/runtime.mjsapi`, and the build stopped with `Could not load`. Every import through those paths failed since v1.10.0, the override stubs of v1.9.3 included. Each pattern now matches the whole path, so those paths reach the names the runtime shim exports. An existing extension takes the fix with a refreshed `vite.extensions.config.ts` (same section). +1 Vitest.
- **The docs imported package internals that an extension build cannot resolve.** 47 imports on 11 pages (`components.md`, `overrides.md`, `differentials.md`, `keyboard-shortcuts.md`, `i18n.md`, `loader.md`, `impersonation.md`, `invitations.md`, `theming.md`, `tools.md`, `troubleshooting.md`) read from `@/lib/*`, `@/hooks/*`, `@/components/*` and `@/contexts/*` as if consumer code were still built inside the package: in a consumer build the legacy aliases send `@/lib/*` and `@/contexts/*` to the runtime shim, which exported none of those names, and every other `@/...` path does not resolve, so 43 of the 44 value imports stopped the build (only `{ api }` from `@/lib/api` compiled). The confirmation-dialog recipe also imported `createPortal` from `react-dom`, which an extension resolves to the React shim (React core only). Each consumer example now imports from `@martis/runtime`; the keyboard-shortcut examples import `addShortcut`, `disableShortcut` and `listShortcuts` from `@martis/runtime`; an example that documents the package's own code says so on a `// Package-internal` line (`QueryErrorState`, the `useEventBus` hook, the `primereact` Tooltip, the shortcut module and its test suite), and the event bus examples use `martisEventBus`. `troubleshooting.md` told the reader to add an import to the extension entry to reach the registry, and `overrides.md` named a `window.componentRegistry` that does not exist: both now point to `window.Martis.componentRegistry.keys()`. Prose that sent extensions to `@/components/metrics`, `@/lib/i18n` and `@/lib/config` (`metrics.md`, `differentials.md`, `i18n.md`, `loader.md`) names the runtime. The docs guard in `martisRuntime.test.tsx` now resolves every import of a docs page through the consumer's alias table and fails on a name the target shim does not export, on an `@/...` or `@martis/...` path no alias resolves, and on a legacy path used in place of `@martis/runtime`. Checked with real consumer builds: the 72 consumer-facing imports of the docs compile with the new shim.
- **The documented way to replace the bundled palette shortcut did nothing.** `keyboard-shortcuts.md` removed `mod+k` from the extension entry, but the extension bundle runs before the SPA mounts and the topbar registers `mod+k` and `/` when it mounts, so there was nothing to remove yet. The recipe now takes the combo over: when several handlers share a combo, the first one registered runs, so a handler the extension entry registers wins over the bundled one (with `allowInInput: true`, like the bundled `mod+k`, or the bundled handler still runs while an input has focus). +1 Vitest.
- **`tsc` could not check a consumer extension: nothing declared the types of `@martis/runtime`.** An extension build resolves `@martis/runtime`, `react-router-dom`, `react-i18next`, `@tanstack/react-query` and the legacy paths to the shims under `resources/js/martis-extensions/.shims/`, but the published `tsconfig.extensions.json` mapped none of them (its only alias, `@ext/*`, was one the Vite config never resolved), so `tsc -p tsconfig.extensions.json` answered `Cannot find module '@martis/runtime'` for every runtime import, the override stubs the generators write included, with cascading errors (`err instanceof ApiError` narrowed nothing). The tsconfig also required `@types/node` (`"types": ["node"]`, TS2688 without it) and set `baseUrl`, which TypeScript 6, the version `martis:install` installs today, rejects as deprecated (TS5101). `npm run build:types` (run by `npm run build`) now generates the declarations of the five shims (`react-dom` included) from the modules the runtime serves (`resources/js/extension-types/`, bundled with `rollup-plugin-dts`: the Martis types and those of the host's third-party libraries inlined, `react`, `react-dom` and `@phosphor-icons/react` left to the consumer); `martis:install` publishes them next to the shims; the tsconfig maps every aliased specifier to them, drops `baseUrl` and `@ext/*`, and covers only the browser-side extension sources (`"types": ["vite/client"]`). The new `martis-extension-shims` tag refreshes an existing app's shims and declarations together (`php artisan vendor:publish --tag=martis-extension-shims --force`), and the installation guide gives the tsconfig change. Declaration emit also needed the three registry classes exported (`ComponentRegistry`, `LayoutRegistry`, `IconRegistry`), whose instances the runtime serves. CI checks that the committed declarations are current and type-checks every generator's output in the fresh-Laravel smoke.
- **A field definition written by hand did not satisfy `FieldDefinition`.** The documented Tool forms (`useMartisForm({ fields })`, `tool-fields.md`) pass definitions with only `type`, `attribute` and `label`, which the client renders, but the type required the nine flags the server fills in (`nullable`, `readonly`, `required`, `sortable`, `searchable`, `showOnIndex`, `showOnDetail`, `showOnForms`, `rules`), and `FieldType` missed 15 of the bundled fields (`slug`, `repeater`, `boolean_group`, `audio`, …) and every custom field. The flags are optional (an absent flag reads as off, and the client never reads `showOn*` or `rules`), and `FieldType` lists every bundled field and accepts a custom field's type; a test compares the list with each PHP field's `type()`.
- **`martisEventBus.on`, `once` and `off` did not type the payload of a built-in event.** Only `emit` had the per-event overload, so a handler that destructured the payload (the `components.md` example) failed to type-check, although the runtime's comments promised typed `.on(...)` payloads. The three take the same overload now; a custom event keeps the untyped signature. The same check of the docs examples found that PrimeReact's `Tooltip` has no `escape` prop: the docs and the runtime comments recommended `escape={false}` for rich content, which did nothing. Rich content is JSX `content` on the ref-based `Tooltip`, or markup on a trigger with `data-pr-tooltip-html="true"`, and the examples and comments now say so.
- **Editors flagged every `@martis/runtime` import in an extension.** VS Code and every other tsserver client type a file with the nearest `tsconfig.json`, which a plain Laravel app does not have and a starter-kit app fills with settings that know none of the extension aliases. `martis:install` now also publishes `resources/js/martis-extensions/tsconfig.json`, which extends `tsconfig.extensions.json`, so the editor resolves the runtime and the shimmed modules as `tsc -p tsconfig.extensions.json` does; Vite's esbuild reads that tsconfig too when it compiles the extension sources: its `target` (ES2022) gives class fields define semantics, so an extension that declares class fields, in an app without a root `tsconfig.json`, now builds them as native class fields (Vite 7) or `__publicField` calls (Vite 6, whose build target is ES2020) instead of assignments in the constructor, and an app whose root `tsconfig.json` covered the extension sources compiles them with the extension tsconfig's settings. Code without class fields builds byte for byte as before.
- **The generated sidebar override drew a nested menu group as a link.** A group's `items` in `/api/navigation` hold nested groups (`type: 'group'`) as well as entries, but the `martis:component --type=sidebar` stub typed them with interfaces of its own that had no groups, so a nested group rendered as a link to nowhere and its items were lost. The stub now types the payload with `NavigationGroup` / `NavigationItem`, new on `@martis/runtime` with `NavigationGroupChild` and `NavigationNestedGroup`, and lists a nested group's items under its label.
- **The override files of v1.9.3 did not type-check.** The field override imports its props from `@/components/fields/types`, which no alias covered (Vite drops the type-only import, `tsc` could not resolve it), and the sidebar override imports `NavigationGroup`, which the runtime did not export. The Vite config and the tsconfig now send that exact path to the runtime, which exports the navigation types; `tsc` then reports the nested-group bug of the old sidebar override, which `martis:component --type=sidebar --force` regenerates.
- **The docs examples that import `@martis/runtime` were never type-checked.** `extensionTypecheck.test.ts` now compiles, as a consumer's `tsc` would against the published declarations and tsconfig, the TSX every generator writes (strict) and every docs block in a ```ts, ```tsx or ```typescript fence that imports `@martis/runtime` or a library the build shims, or uses `window.Martis` (a block headed `// resources/js/martis-extensions/index.ts` compiled as that file) (only the context a snippet leaves out is ignored). It found three `components.md` examples without their React imports and the confirmation-dialog recipe typing its props with an undeclared `Props`, both fixed. The Tailwind scan now skips test files, so a token in a test (`!block` in a condition) can no longer add a utility to the published CSS.
- **Fields ignored `colSpanMd()` / `colSpanLg()`, Panel and Tab grids never collapsed on phones, and the filter panel stayed 12 columns at every viewport.** `grid-layout.md` documents `colSpanMd()` (>= 768px) and `colSpanLg()` (>= 1024px) and `Field::toArray()` serialises them, but the Section, Panel and Tab bodies only wrote `grid-column: span {colSpan}` inline (the create and update drawers wrote the same no-op declaration on their loose fields), only the Section grid collapsed below `md` (through an `!important` rule), and `FilterPanel` fixed `repeat(12, …)` and `grid-column: span N` inline, so a `span(3)` filter was a quarter of a phone screen. Placement now follows the v1.37.2 dashboard grid: every form and detail field grid carries `.martis-field-grid` and its track count as `--martis-field-columns` (`Section::columns()`, 12 for a Panel or a Tab), each field its resolved tiers as `--martis-field-span`, `-md` and `-lg` (`lib/fieldGridSpan.ts`, cascade `colSpan()` → `colSpanMd()` → `colSpanLg()`), and `martis.css` owns `grid-column` per media query: full row below `md` on every field grid, the md tier from 768px, the lg tier from 1024px. A field that only sets `colSpan()` renders as before from `md`. The filter panel gets `.martis-filter-grid` and `--martis-filter-span` (`lib/filterGridSpan.ts`): full row below `md`, its span from there. No inline `grid-column` is left, so a theme overrides any tier with a plain rule instead of `!important`. Every tier is also clamped to the grid's tracks: a field without `span()` in a `Section::columns(3)` grid (serialised `colSpan` 12) made the browser add nine implicit columns, squeezing the real ones and placing a fourth `span(1)` field in a few-pixel column; it now takes the full row, as documented. +27 Vitest, +9 Pest. See [Grid Layout → Responsiveness](docs/grid-layout.md#responsiveness) and [Filters → Filter Grid Layout](docs/filters.md#filter-grid-layout-span).
- **With `confirmUnsavedChanges()` on, Back and Forward misbehaved around the create and edit pages.** The full-page guard pushed a history entry every time a create or edit form mounted, also when the page was reached by Back or Forward, so each visit erased the Forward history. With a clean form it took every Back press for its own entry and went back once more: from the index, edit A, then a link to edit B, Back showed A but lost B from Forward, the next Back changed nothing, and the one after skipped the index or left the app while the address bar and the page on screen disagreed. It also swallowed the Back press of a modal open over the form. The guard now pushes its entry only once the form has unsaved changes (a copy of the page's own entry, router key and index included), acts only on the Back press that removes it, and hands every other pop to the router or to the modal or drawer on top. A page left with that entry on top is walked past in one press when Back returns to it. The overrides docs no longer list a `beforeunload` prompt the guard never had. Same on v1.37.3. +9 Vitest. See [Overrides → Unsaved changes guard](docs/overrides.md#unsaved-changes-guard).
- **Text typed while a save ran counted as saved.** The update page refreshed the unsaved-changes baseline from the form's values when the PUT succeeded, not from what it had sent, and the inputs stay editable during the request. After "Save & continue editing" or a `redirectAfterUpdate()` to the record's own edit page, what was typed meanwhile was dropped without a prompt when the user left. The update drawer never moved its baseline, so a drawer its host keeps open (`redirectAfter` `STAY`) asked to discard the changes it had just saved. Both now take the values the save sent as the new baseline (the drawer only for the record they were sent for). Same on v1.37.3 for "Save & continue editing". +4 Vitest. See [Resources → Form save variants](docs/resources.md#form-save-variants).
- **"Create & add another" carried the previous record's state into the next one.** The page cleared the values under the mounted inputs, and an input tells a value from outside by comparing it with its last one, which fails when the value returns to where it was: a `Slug` the user had emptied by hand stopped following the next title, a `Sparkline` kept the previous parse error and rewrote the user's own text as they typed (`[1, 2]` became `[1,2]`), and a custom input that reads its value at mount kept the previous record's value. After replicating a record, the emptied form counted as unsaved. The create page now mounts the fields again for the next record, with the empty form as the guard's baseline; `Slug` follows its source again once both are empty, `Sparkline` adopts only values it did not emit, and `Repeater` tells the values it receives apart by a token rather than by identity, so an emptied Repeater never takes the cleared form for its own rows. +12 Vitest. See [Overrides → Input Component](docs/overrides.md#input-component).
- **A create drawer kept open after a create carried the previous record into the next one.** A host that keeps the create drawer open (`redirectAfter('stay')`) got the form cleared under the mounted inputs, so an input with state of its own kept the previous record's, and a drawer opened on a copy asked to discard the emptied form. It now mounts its fields again and counts the empty form as clean. +2 Vitest. See [Overrides → Override constructor](docs/overrides.md#override-constructor--redirectafter).
- **The inline-create modal reopened with the text typed the last time.** It emptied its form one render after its fields had mounted again, so an input that reads its value when it mounts kept the text of the record the user had given up on. It now empties its form as it closes. +1 Vitest. See [Fields → Inline Create](docs/fields.md#inline-create).
- **The relation pickers of pivot fields and Repeater rows opened empty.** A `BelongsTo`, `MorphTo` or `Tag` among the pivot fields of a `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` rendered in the attach and pivot edit modals with no scope, so it asked the parent resource's relatable endpoint, which reads the parent's forms, where no pivot field is declared (404). It now asks the panel: `GET /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/{belongs-to-many|morph-to-many}/{relationship}/pivot-fields/relatable/{field}` in the attach modal and `.../pivot-fields/{relatedId}/relatable/{field}` in the pivot edit modal, which find the field in the relationship's `fields()` only and answer through the relatable pipeline (its `relatableQueryUsing()`, the parent resource's `relatable{PluralModelName}()`, search). They are gated like the panel (`viewAny`, the parent through `indexQuery()`, `view`, a declared relationship of the route's type), then like the attach (`attachAny{Model}`) or the pivot update of a record the relationship attaches (`updatePivot{Model}`); the modals pass a new `pivotEndpoint` input prop. A picker in a `Repeater` row got the form's scope with `context="update"` forced, and the form lookup never opened a Repeater (404, or the options of a form field reusing the attribute), while a remote `Select` in a row answered 422. Row fields now get the form's own context and scope plus a new `repeaterRow` prop, and name their row with `?repeater={attribute}&repeatable={type}`; every relatable endpoint (form, Action, pivot action, pivot fields) and both `Select` option searches read the field from that row, behind their own gates. +61 Pest, +17 Vitest. See [Relationships → Relation pickers among the pivot fields](docs/relationships.md#relation-pickers-among-the-pivot-fields) and [Repeater → Relation pickers and remote selects in rows](docs/repeater.md#relation-pickers-and-remote-selects-in-rows).
- **`readonly()` and `immutable()` left the inline-create "+" of a `BelongsTo` or `MorphTo` live.** Both inputs disabled their pickers but built `canShowCreateButton` without `field.readonly`, so on an update form (an `immutable()` field arrives as `readonly`) and on the parent `BelongsTo` a nested create locks, the "+" of `showCreateRelationButton()` created a real record and selected it: the update dropped the new value (200, column unchanged) and left a record nothing points to, and the nested create showed another parent while the server still created the record under the one in the URL. A readonly field now renders neither the "+" nor its modal, and a record the modal creates after the field turned readonly leaves the value alone. Same on v1.37.3 for `readonly()`. +9 Vitest. See [Fields → Immutable fields](docs/fields.md#immutable-fields).
- **The `MorphTo` inline-create "+" showed for a type the user may not create.** The field serialises `showCreateRelationButton: true` as soon as one of its types is creatable and flags each `morphTypes` entry with its `authorizedToCreate`, but the input never read the per-type flag: after picking a type the user may not create, the "+" opened a modal that failed with a 403 on its schema. The "+" now follows the picked type. Same on v1.37.3. +2 Vitest. See [Fields → MorphTo](docs/fields.md#morphto).
- **The create drawer kept the previous target's values, and the index page kept its overlays when it moved to another resource.** `DrawerCreate` seeded its values and dirty baseline once, at mount, so a host that swaps the target without remounting it (`ActionDrawer` when an action response opens another resource's create drawer while one is open, the detail page moving its Replicate drawer to another record, the index page moving to another resource's index, which the command palette does over any drawer) posted what was typed for the first target to the second. It now seeds its form again (values, validation errors, dirty baseline, fields mounted afresh) when the resource or the replicated record changes; a fresh copy of the same record keeps the edits. The index page reset its list but kept its create drawer, the drawers opened from its rows or by an action, the action modal and the delete, restore and force-delete confirmations open: the delete confirmation of a row became "Delete {new resource}" and deleted the record with the same id there. It now closes them when its resource changes. Same on v1.37.3. +8 Vitest. See [Overrides → Override Props](docs/overrides.md#override-props).
- **The detail page's confirmations followed the page to another record.** The router keeps the detail page when the URL moves to another record (the command palette opens over a confirmation), and the delete, force-delete and restore confirmations and the action modal stayed open: confirming a delete opened for `posts/3` after moving to `posts/4` deleted `posts/4`. They now close when the record changes; the update and Replicate drawers keep following the record in the URL. Same on v1.37.3. +1 Vitest. See [Overrides → Override Props](docs/overrides.md#override-props).
- **Switching lenses kept the previous lens's state, and the new lens opened without its default filters.** The router keeps the lens page when the URL moves to another lens (the page's own lens dropdown) or to another resource's lens, so `withDefaultFilters()` had already been seeded, the selection, drawers and delete confirmation of the previous lens stayed, and a search typed just before the move landed in the new lens's URL. The page now starts over for each lens; the view state lives in the URL. Same on v1.37.3. +3 Vitest. See [Lenses → Default filters pre-applied](docs/lenses.md#default-filters-pre-applied).
- **The dead `BelongsTo` multiple mode is gone.** `BelongsTo::multiple()` was removed in April 2026 (a N:N relation is a `BelongsToMany` or a `Tag`), but `BelongsToField.tsx` kept the mode, and `withMeta(['multiple' => true])` still switched it on: the display showed "—" for a stored record, and the input became a multi-select whose list of ids `BelongsTo::fill()` read as no id, so saving cleared the foreign key. Both now only know the single mode, and the unused `belongs_to_multiple_selected` translation is dropped. The `DeferredRelationSync` docblock (and an orphaned one in `ResourceController`) no longer claim `BelongsTo` registers syncs: only `Tag` does. +2 Vitest (2 that only covered the multiple mode removed). See [Fields → BelongsTo](docs/fields.md#belongsto).
- **`Tag::showCreateRelationButton()` did nothing.** The field serialised the flag and `modalSize()`, but the picker never read them, so a missing tag could only be created by leaving the form. The picker's dropdown now ends with a *Create* entry that opens the related resource's inline-create modal and adds the new record to the selection (a preloaded option list is fetched again). The entry follows the related resource's policy (`Tag::isShowCreateRelationButton()` now checks `authorizedToCreate`, as `BelongsTo` does), is absent on a readonly field, and the inline-create schema turns it off for a `Tag` inside another inline-create form. +4 Vitest, +3 Pest. See [Fields → Tag](docs/fields.md#tag).
- **`Tag::withPreview()` did nothing.** The flag was serialised and ignored. A tag on the index and detail pages now opens its record's peek card after a short hover: the related resource's `fieldsForPreview()`, the card a `BelongsTo` shows. +2 Vitest. See [Fields → Tag](docs/fields.md#tag).
- **`relationSearchable(false)` did nothing on the `BelongsTo`, `MorphTo` and `Tag` pickers.** It turns off a relationship panel's search box, and the three fields serialise it, but their pickers always showed one. A picker without search now lists its options with no search box, up to 100 of them (the relatable endpoint's cap, or the related resource's `$relatableSearchResults`). `troubleshooting.md` also claimed the method makes the global search issue a `whereHas`; it now points to `searchableRelations()`. +6 Vitest. See [Fields → BelongsTo](docs/fields.md#belongsto).
- **A Replicate through a create override copied the record's detail payload, and no Replicate showed `replicatedMessage()`.** The detail page opens a resource's create override (the bundled `DrawerCreate`, or a custom one) for the Replicate action with the record it shows, and `DrawerCreate` copied that detail payload into the form: stored File paths (the copy would point at the same file), fields the replicate endpoint leaves out, and no `authorizedToReplicate()` check, while the create page reads `GET /api/resources/{resource}/{id}/replicate`. A create override opened by `/create?fromResourceId={id}` got no copy at all. `OverrideProps` now carries `fromResourceId` on a create override, and `DrawerCreate` reads the copy from the replicate endpoint, mounts its fields once it has arrived, shows the endpoint's error instead of a form, and sends `fromResourceId` with its first create. `Resource::replicatedMessage()` was documented as the toast after a Replicate but never shown: the store now answers a create that carries `fromResourceId` with it, and the hosts of a create override toast it too. +5 Vitest, +1 Pest. See [Overrides → Override Props](docs/overrides.md#override-props) and [Fields → Resource Replication](docs/fields.md#resource-replication).
- **Repeater rows are validated on the server, and their errors show under the row fields.** The rules of the fields inside a `Repeatable` never reached the validator, although `docs/repeater.md` said every row was validated, so a row with a missing or invalid value was stored as sent. Every endpoint that writes a Repeater (resource create and update, JSON and multipart, the inline create, the `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` inline forms, the pivot attach and update, an Action's fields) now validates each row with the fields of its Repeatable under `{attribute}.{row}.fields.{field}`, named by the field's label ("The Key field is required."). `required` holds on update too, since a save replaces every row; `creationRules()` / `updateRules()` follow the parent write; readonly, computed and file fields and the `unique()` helper are left out; a row whose `type` names no Repeatable fails on `{attribute}.{row}.type`. `Repeater::buildRowValidation()` exposes the same rules to custom controllers. Every form now hands an input the errors inside its value (`nestedErrors`), so the Repeater shows each row error under its row field (it follows its row when rows move, a collapsed row opens, editing the field clears it) and shows its own error, which it never did. +27 Pest, +26 Vitest. See [Repeater → Validation](docs/repeater.md#validation).
- **The `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` inline forms saved a record without the values written after the save.** Only `ResourceController` flushed the queue a `Tag` field and a `HasMany` or polymorphic `Repeater` fill until the record has a key, so a record created or updated through an inline form (the create form a relation panel opens included) lost its tags and its rows. Every controller now flushes it right after the save (`Http\Controllers\Concerns\SyncsDeferredWrites`). The inline forms also apply the fields' custom validation messages, such as a `unique()` message. Covered by the Repeater validation tests above.
- **The docs described validation errors that no endpoint returns.** `docs/api/overview.md` showed Laravel's keyed 422 for the resource endpoints, which answer with the Martis `{ field, message, code }` list, and the `docs/tool-fields.md` example fed that list to `setErrors()` as a map, so no error reached a field; it now passes `ApiError.errorsByField()`. `minRows()` / `maxRows()` no longer claim a server check they never had: `rules(['array', 'min:N', 'max:N'])` is the documented way.
- **A create form opened from a relationship panel lost the files it carried.** The form posts to the relationship's endpoint (`/api/resources/{parent}/{id}/{has-many|…}/{relationship}`) and always sent JSON, where a picked file serialises to `{}`, while the same form opened on its own sends multipart when it carries a file. It now uploads the same way. +2 Vitest. See [Relationships](docs/relationships.md#which-record-a-panel-belongs-to).
- **A Markdown or Trix field crashed the page when its value went from empty to set, as did an Icon input whose field became stored.** Their displays (and the Icon input) called some of their hooks only after the early return for an empty value (or a display-only Icon field), so the same element called a different number of hooks once the value was set or cleared (a detail page refetch, polling, an inline edit) and React threw "Rendered more hooks than during the previous render". Every hook now runs on every render. The ESLint `rules-of-hooks` rule, which flagged them, is an error now, and CI fails on any lint warning. +5 Vitest.
- **Editing a Trix field in a Repeater row put back the row's other fields, and a second Trix editor for the same attribute edited the first one's content.** The editor reported every edit through the `onChange` of the render that mounted it, so text typed into the row's other fields since then was lost, and its hidden input was named after the attribute alone, so the next Repeater row (or an inline-create modal over a form with the same field) loaded and wrote the first editor's input. The editor now reports through the latest `onChange` and owns a uniquely named input; under StrictMode (the dev server) it reports edits again. +4 Vitest.
- **Hooks read stale values or re-ran for nothing.** A `BelongsTo` / `MorphTo` picker kept the options of its previous scope when the scope changed while its dropdown was open; the two-factor setup wizard, the browser sessions list, the profile page and the two-factor challenge re-ran effects (a new 2FA secret per render in the wizard, a restarted countdown in the challenge) or read an outdated translation; the timezone picker's clock only advanced through a dependency the lint flagged. Each hook now lists what it reads, or reads the latest value through a ref where re-running would be wrong. +6 Vitest.
- **The keyboard-shortcut recipes did not type-check in an extension.** `keyboard-shortcuts.md` called `window.Martis.shortcuts`, but the only `Window.Martis` a consumer program declares is the one in the scaffold's `index.ts`, which knows `componentRegistry` alone, so every recipe failed with TS18048 and TS2339, "Take over a bundled shortcut" included, although it goes into that very file. `addShortcut`, `disableShortcut` and `listShortcuts` are on `@martis/runtime` now (runtime, shim and declarations, with the `ShortcutOptions` and `Shortcut` types), and the recipes import them from there. The SPA's own `Window.Martis` declaration moved to an ambient `martis-global.d.ts`: left in `keyboardShortcuts.ts`, the runtime declarations would carry it and clash (TS2717) with the entry's own as soon as `index.ts` imports the runtime. The docs type-check also compiles the ```typescript blocks, the blocks that import a shimmed library or use `window.Martis`, a block headed `// resources/js/martis-extensions/index.ts` as that file, and the entry itself once it imports the runtime. +1 Vitest. See [Keyboard shortcuts](docs/keyboard-shortcuts.md).
- **`import { createPortal } from 'react-dom'` type-checked in an extension and then failed the build.** The Vite config sent `react-dom` to the React shim, which exports React core only, and the tsconfig had no `paths` entry for it, so `tsc` read the app's `@types/react-dom` and the build stopped with `"createPortal" is not exported by ".shims/react.mjs"`. `react-dom` now goes to a shim of its own, `.shims/react-dom.mjs`, which re-exports the runtime's `createPortal` (and a default export holding it), and the tsconfig maps it to that shim's declarations, which inline the host's: any other `react-dom` name fails `tsc` as it fails the build. `martis:install` and the `martis-extension-shims` tag publish both files; an existing app also needs the new alias and `paths` entry (installation guide). +3 Vitest. See [Installation](docs/installation-guide.md#refreshing-the-extension-scaffold-after-an-upgrade).
- **The declarations of the third-party shims exported none of their library's types.** `import type { UseQueryResult } from '@tanstack/react-query'`, or `LinkProps` / `NavigateFunction` from `react-router-dom`, failed with TS2614, and an app that had installed a library for its types lost them, since the tsconfig `paths` send the specifier to the declarations. Each declaration now exports every interface and type alias of the host's copy of its library (74 for react-router-dom, 17 for react-i18next, 148 for @tanstack/react-query, 2 for react-dom); a class or enum the shim does not export as a value (`QueryCache`, `NavigationType`) stays out, and the default export, the host's module, still holds it. The docs guard checks type imports against those declarations instead of skipping them. +1 Vitest.
- **`martis:list-overrides --frontend` reported registered keys as missing.** It only walked the four buckets, so a key registered by hand in `index.ts` (the check `troubleshooting.md` describes) showed as missing and the command exited 2, and so did every resource row, whose URI key nothing registers, which failed the documented CI use on any app with a resource. The command now also reads the literal key of each `register()` call on the component registry in `index.ts` (comments, computed keys and the layout and icon registries skipped) and shows a resource as `n/a`. `overrides.md` no longer documents the `--boot` flag removed in v1.10 or a parser of `registerFieldDisplay` calls. +5 Pest.
- **The docs contradicted themselves on rich tooltip content.** `components.md`, the runtime's comments and the runtime shim said rich content must go through the ref-based `Tooltip`, while the global provider renders any trigger with `data-pr-tooltip-html="true"` as HTML (the field label tooltips, the metric help and the cache page use it), and `differentials.md` and `fields.md` said only the label renderer sets it. Every page now gives both routes and when to take each: `data-pr-tooltip-html="true"` for markup you write (not sanitised, so never user or record data), the ref-based `Tooltip` with JSX `content` for React content. `differentials.md` also gave the first-hover delay as 300 ms; it is 500 ms. See [Components → Tooltips](docs/components.md).
- **`martis:install` gave a new app the React 19 types for extensions that run on React 18.** An extension's `react` resolves to a shim of the host's React 18.3, but `@types/react` and `@types/react-dom` were added at `^18 || ^19`, so `import { use, useOptimistic } from 'react'` type-checked and built (the React shim exports those names) and was undefined in the browser. Both are added at `^18`, the major of the package's `react`; an app with its own `@types/react` keeps it, and the installation guide says what the React 19 types let through. +1 Pest.
- **A field `martis:field` generated, or a card `martis:card` generated, never rendered its component.** The extension entry registered `fields/PriceTag.tsx` as `field:price-tag`, a key the field renderer never reads (it resolves a field by `type()`: `field:display:price-tag` / `field:input:price-tag`), and dropped its `Input`; it registered `cards/RevenueGauge.tsx` as `card:revenue-gauge` while the generated card bound `componentKey('revenue-gauge')`, and the dashboard resolves a card by that exact key. The generators also derived keys with `Str::kebab()`, which splits an acronym (`SEOReport` became `s-e-o-report`) where the entry keeps it whole (`seo-report`). The entry now registers a field module as the display and input of its type, `martis:card` binds `componentKey('card:<name>')`, the generators and `martis:list-overrides` derive keys with `ExtensionKey::kebab()` (a port of the entry's `pascalToKebab()`), and `martis:tool --with-component --component-key=…` prints the `register()` call `index.ts` needs. The installation guide says what an app generated earlier has to change. +10 Vitest, +11 Pest.
- **The installation guide left the editor tsconfig out of the scaffold it lists.** The three lists of what `martis:install` copies and what `--force` rewrites omitted `resources/js/martis-extensions/tsconfig.json`, and the Install Options table said `--force` overwrites the published config, which only `--force-config` does; the table now lists `--force-config` and `--force-provider` too.
- **PHP 8.5 logged a deprecation on every boot.** The service provider's reset-password and verify-email hooks, the tool provider and the MCP HTTP transport called `ReflectionProperty::setAccessible()` / `ReflectionMethod::setAccessible()`, which has had no effect since PHP 8.1 and is deprecated in PHP 8.5. The calls are gone.
- **A detach that failed said nothing, and the many-to-many modals left their errors unhandled.** A `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` detach the server refused (a 403 when the policy denies it, a 500) left the confirmation open with no message: the mutation had no error handler and the confirmation awaited it without catching. The confirmation now says why the detach failed. The attach and pivot update modals fired their mutations as unawaited promises, so every 422 they already displayed also raised an unhandled rejection. +2 Vitest.
- **A Tool form built for a stored record rendered its inputs as a create form.** `useMartisForm({ context: 'update' })` locked the immutable fields, but `fieldProps()` left the context out and `FieldsForm` defaulted its own to `'create'`, so an input that behaves differently on an edit form (a `Slug` keeps a stored slug, a Repeater locks the immutable fields of its stored rows) behaved as on a create form. `fieldProps()` now carries the form's context, the form exposes it as `context`, and `FieldsForm` defaults to it. +2 Vitest. See [Tool fields](docs/tool-fields.md).
- **A parameter sent as an array where a string is expected answered 500.** `(string)` on an array raises "Array to string conversion", which Laravel turns into an exception: `?context[]=` on the action lists, `email[]=` / `token[]=` on the magic-link consume, `type[]=` on the cache admin endpoints, `?range[]=` / `?filters[]=` on a metric, and `email[]=` on any login route, since the `martis-login` rate limiter reads it before validation. Each is now read as a string or treated as missing, so the request gets its normal answer (the login's 422, the magic link's invalid-link redirect, the cache endpoints' 422). +5 Pest.
- **The attach modal offered an input for a pivot field a new row hides.** A pivot field whose `canSeeForModel()` hides it on the row the attach writes still rendered in the attach modal of a `BelongsToMany` / `MorphToMany` panel, although the attach ignores what the form sends for it and stores its `default()`. The list of records to attach now names those fields (`meta.hiddenPivotFields`), and the modal leaves them out. +2 Pest, +2 Vitest. See [Fields → Field authorization](docs/fields.md#field-authorization-cansee-and-canseeformodel).
- **The action modal took a dry run for a finished run and never showed the preview.** `withDryRun()` adds a Preview button that posts `dryRun: true`, and the server answers the action's `dryRun()` preview without running it, but `ActionModal` handled that answer like a run: success toast, modal closed, list refreshed, and nothing had run; the pivot action modal had no Preview button, and an action with no fields and no confirmation ran before Preview could be offered. Both modals now show the preview (`ActionDryRunPreview`: its `preview` text, then every other key) and stay open for Run, a field change clears it, and a dry-run action always opens its modal. +3 Vitest. See [Actions → Dry-Run Preview](docs/actions.md#dry-run-preview-martis-extension).
- **`theming.md` said the PrimeReact variable bridge restyles PrimeReact components.** The bundled `lara-dark-indigo` theme paints with literal colours and reads only `--font-family`, `--font-feature-settings` and `--maskbg`, so setting `--primary-color` alone restyles no component: the selector overrides in `martis.css` are what make the components Martis renders follow the `--martis-*` tokens. The troubleshooting entry now says so and shows how to restyle a PrimeReact element Martis does not cover. See [Theming → Some colors don't change](docs/theming.md#some-colors-dont-change).

Assets rebuilt.

## [1.37.3] — 2026-09-21

### Fixed

- **The multipart request path stringified structured values and booleans.** As soon as a form uploaded a file the SPA switched the whole submission to `multipart/form-data` and `buildFormData()` appended every non-file value with `String(val)`: a JSON-mode `Repeater`'s rows arrived as `"[object Object],[object Object]"` (rows silently emptied), a `MultiSelect`'s selection as `"12,15"` (cleared, or 422 on a literal `array` rule), a `BooleanGroup`'s map as `"[object Object]"` (stored as text, every later read of the record 500ed), a `Tag`'s ids as `"1,2"` (tags detached), a `MorphTo`'s target as `"[object Object]"` (ignored) and an unchecked `Boolean` as `"false"`, which PHP's `(bool)` reads as `true`. `buildFormData()` now sends booleans as `1` / `0` and arrays or plain objects as `JSON.stringify(val)`; on the server, `Field::hasStructuredValue()` (`true` on `Repeater`, `MultiSelect`, `BooleanGroup`, `KeyValue`, `Tag`, `MorphTo`, `Sparkline`; override it on a custom field) marks the fields whose form value is a list or a map, and the new `DecodesStructuredValues` trait, run by `validateRequest()` in `ResourceController` and the `HasMany` / `HasOne` / `MorphMany` / `MorphOne` controllers, decodes a JSON-string value of such a field back to the array before the rules run and `fill()` receives it, so the multipart path sees exactly what the JSON path sends and a string that does not decode to an array fails validation instead of being stored. `BooleanGroup::fill()` decodes a JSON string into the map, the mirror of what its `resolve()` already reads. `buildFormData()` is exported and unit-tested. +11 Pest, +4 Vitest. See [Fields → Structured values and file uploads](docs/fields.md#structured-values-and-file-uploads).
- **`MultiSelect`, `KeyValue` and multiple-file `File` / `Image` double-encoded an `array` / `json` / class-cast attribute.** Their `fill()` `json_encode()`d the list or map themselves before `setAttribute()`, so on an attribute with a JSON-family or class cast Eloquent's `set()` step encoded that string a second time: the column held a JSON string *of* a JSON string and every later read through the cast returned a string instead of an array, from the first save through the admin form on. `Field` gains `modelSerialisesStructuredValue()` (mirrors Eloquent's own `isJsonCastable()` / `isClassCastable()`: the native JSON-family cast list plus any cast whose caster segment names a class, so `AsCollection::using(...)` and `AsEnumCollection::of(...)` are covered without enumerating class names) and `storableStructuredValue()`; the four fills route their write through it, handing the cast the PHP array to serialise once, while an uncast column keeps receiving the single-encoded JSON string it always did. +16 Pest. See [Fields → Structured values and Eloquent casts](docs/fields.md#structured-values-and-eloquent-casts).
- **`Repeatable::title(Closure)` was never resolved.** The Closure form of `title()` was serialised as `hasTitleCallback: true` but `resolveTitle()` had no caller and the frontend only branched on `titleTemplate`, so every row of a Closure-titled repeatable fell back to the static label. `Repeater::resolve()` now attaches the Closure's result to each row as `title` in all three storage modes (JSON, HasMany, polymorphic), computed against the row's field map and its 1-based index as the API documents; rows of a repeatable without a Closure keep their `{id, type, fields}` shape, `Repeater::fill()` drops the derived key before writing JSON rows so it is never stored, and `Repeatable` gains `hasTitleCallback()`. The SPA prefers the resolved `title`, keeps evaluating `{attr}` templates live, falls back to "Label #N" for a Closure-titled row without a resolved title yet (added or edited in the form; the next save refreshes it), and renders the `#N` badge only next to a static label so the row number no longer shows twice with `badgeCount()`. The legacy flat-row detection in `normalizeJsonRow()` compared `$type` against `null` after it had already received the first repeatable's `shortName`, so it never matched while repeatables were registered; it now checks the raw `fields` / `type` keys. +10 Pest, +10 Vitest. See [Repeater → Row header affordances](docs/repeater.md#row-header-affordances).
- **`Image::fillMultiple()` kept any client-supplied `existing` path.** `File::fillMultiple()` only honours `existing` paths the record already owns (the ecosystem-audit guard covered by `AuditSecurityHighsTest`), but the `Image` counterpart shipped without it, so a tampered multiple-image payload could inject arbitrary disk paths (another record's uploads, a traversal) into the stored set. `Image` now applies the same guard: an injected path is dropped, and an owned path the client omits is deleted together with its thumbnail. +2 Pest. See [Fields → Image](docs/fields.md#image).
- **The nested-dashboard tab strip did not switch dashboards.** `DashboardPage` derives the current dashboard from the `/dashboards/:uriKey` route parameter first, and the tabs' `onSelect` only wrote a `useState` that this derivation shadowed whenever the page was opened at a deep link, the only way to reach a non-root family, so clicking a child tab changed nothing (no URL, no request, no active state). The tabs are now links to each dashboard's own address (`/` for the first registered dashboard, as the sidebar already does, otherwise `/dashboards/{uriKey}`), the dead selection state is gone, and `DashboardView` is keyed on the dashboard so the active filters start empty after a switch; Enter / Space, middle-click and open-in-new-tab work, and the strip carries `tablist` / `tab` roles and `aria-current`. +5 Vitest. See [Dashboards → Nested dashboards](docs/dashboards.md).

Assets rebuilt.

## [1.37.2] — 2026-09-21

### Fixed

- **The dashboard grid ignored `widthMd()` / `widthLg()` and never collapsed on narrow viewports.** `Metric::toArray()` serialised the two responsive widths (documented since the grid was introduced) but the SPA wrote `grid-column: span {width}` inline on every card and fixed the container at `repeat(12, …)` at every viewport, so a mobile-first declaration (`width(12)->widthMd(12)->widthLg(8)`) rendered the base width everywhere and a plain `width(4)` card was a third of a 390px screen. Placement now belongs to `.martis-dashboard-grid` in `martis.css`: each card carries its resolved tiers as custom properties (`--martis-card-span`, `-md`, `-lg`, from `lib/cardGridSpan.ts`, cascade `width()` → `widthMd()` → `widthLg()` with each tier inheriting the previous one) and the stylesheet applies them per media query, `md` = 768px and `lg` = 1024px, the same breakpoints as the form grid. Below `md` every card spans the full row whatever its declared widths, the rule `.martis-section-grid` already applies to field spans and what Nova's `md:grid-cols-12` does; a card declaring only `width(6)` is therefore unchanged above `md` and full-width on phones. A theme that wants `width()` honoured on phones can override the mobile rule with `grid-column: span var(--martis-card-span)`. A plain custom `Card` gains the same `widthMd()` / `widthLg()` methods (serialised next to `width`, `null` until declared) so both card kinds share one grid contract, and the `martis:card` TSX stub no longer writes a no-op inline `grid-column`. Assets rebuilt. +6 Pest, +10 Vitest. See [Metrics → Responsive widths](docs/metrics.md#responsive-widths-widthmd--widthlg).

## [1.37.1] — 2026-09-21

### Fixed

- **Placeholders of unclassed inputs were white on the light theme.** The bundled PrimeReact theme (`lara-dark-indigo`) ships unscoped placeholder rules (`::-webkit-input-placeholder { color: rgba(255,255,255,.6) }`, and the `-moz` / `-ms` variants), right for its dark palette and invisible on a light surface, and `martis.css` re-coloured only the placeholders of its own controls, each by class (`.p-inputtext`, `.martis-input`, `.martis-search-input`, the ⌘K search, the resource search). A plain `<input placeholder="…">` or `<textarea>` in a Tool page or an extension bundle therefore rendered with no visible placeholder on the light theme, looked like an empty control, and could not be fixed from the consumer side (pseudo-elements are not addressable inline). The package CSS now restores a theme-aware default for every placeholder, `::placeholder { color: var(--martis-text-muted); opacity: 1 }`, unlayered so it beats the theme's `@layer primereact` rule regardless of specificity; the per-class rules keep winning over it, so Martis controls are unchanged in both modes, and a consumer theme that redefines `--martis-text-muted` re-colours bare placeholders too. Assets rebuilt. +3 Pest. See [Theming → Placeholder text in custom controls](docs/theming.md#placeholder-text-in-custom-controls-v1371).

## [1.37.0] — 2026-09-20

### Added

- **`Select::searchableOptions()`, `allowCustomValues()` and `searchOptionsUsing()`: searchable, editable and server-searched selects.** The native `select` mounted the PrimeReact Dropdown without `filter` or `editable`, so an administrator picking one of hundreds of server-provided options (an LLM provider's model list) either scrolled the whole list or the consumer left the field system for a raw `Dropdown`. Three opt-in flags close the gap: `searchableOptions()` renders a search box over the options (label or value; this is what Nova's `Select::searchable()` does, kept apart from Martis's `searchable()`, the column-search flag), `allowCustomValues()` accepts a typed value outside the list (stored as-is, rendered raw on index and detail, no validation added), and `searchOptionsUsing(fn (string $term, ?Request $request) => [...])` searches the options on the server as the user types through two new endpoints, `GET /api/resources/{resource}/fields/{field}/options` (gated on the create or update ability of the context, like `sync-field`) and `GET /api/tools/{uriKey}/fields/{field}/options` (404 when `canSee()` denies).
